# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Project

`interop-tools` (formerly `hl7-tools`, renamed as scope expanded beyond HL7v2) is a Python/FastAPI web app for converting healthcare messages/documents to FHIR R4 Bundles. The long-term goal (see README.md) covers transformation, validation, deduplication, test-data generation, and mapping across HL7v2/FHIR/CDA/C-CDA/EDI. Currently implemented:
- ADT — **A01 Admit, A02 Transfer, A03 Discharge, A04 Register, A05 Pre-admit, A08 Update, A11 Cancel Admit, A13 Cancel Discharge, A38 Cancel Pre-Admit** — each converting to a FHIR Bundle (Patient + Encounter).
- SIU — **S12 New booking, S13 Reschedule, S14 Modify, S15 Cancel, S17 Delete, S26 Patient No-Show** — each converting to a FHIR Bundle (Patient + Appointment + real Practitioner/Location/Device resources for the appointment's AIP/AIL/AIG participants, referenced by `urn:uuid`, not left as display-only text).
- ORU — **R01 Observation result, R30/R31/R32/R40 point-of-care result** — each converting to a FHIR Bundle (Patient + optional Encounter + one DiagnosticReport per OBR + one Observation per OBX, correctly grouped so each report only references its own results).
- MDM — **T02 New document, T04 Document status update, T06 Document addendum, T08 Document edit, T10 Document replacement, T11 Document cancel** — each converting to a FHIR Bundle (Patient + optional Encounter + DocumentReference + Binary for the document's OBX-carried text content), TXA → DocumentReference per the v2-to-FHIR IG.
- A synthetic HL7 message generator (`app/generators/`) covering all 26 combinations above, with realistic field-level randomization, selectable from a UI dropdown or `/api/generate`.
- A message **validator** - independent of conversion, runs against any message/document and returns a report of `error`/`warning`/`info` findings, each pointing at the offending location, covering both structural correctness and healthcare data-quality plausibility (a birth date in the future, a discharge before an admit, an appointment ending before it starts). Reachable via the **Validate** button in the UI or `/api/validate`, dispatched by input format via `app/pipeline.py::validate_any` - `app/validation/` for HL7v2, `app/cda/validation.py` for C-CDA.
- **C-CDA → FHIR** (`app/cda/`) - the app's first non-HL7v2 *input format* (XML, not pipe-delimited). Three document types: **CCD** (Continuity of Care Document), **Discharge Summary**, and **History and Physical Note**, all sharing the same header (Patient + optional Encounter) and section-dispatch infrastructure via `app/cda/common.py::build_sectioned_bundle` - seven general sections recognized on any of the three document types: **Problems** → `Condition`, **Medications** → `MedicationRequest`, **Allergies** → `AllergyIntolerance`, **Immunizations** → `Immunization` (administered/refused entries only - see the C-CDA subsection for the disclosed planned-immunization scope limit), **Vital Signs** → an `Observation` panel (`.hasMember`) wrapping one `Observation` per vital sign, **Results** → `DiagnosticReport` (`.result`) wrapping one `Observation` per result, and **Procedures** → `Procedure` - each of the last three registered under both its "entries required" and "entries optional" templateId variants (see the C-CDA subsection's `procedures.py` bullet for the real, evidenced gap this closed) - plus two Discharge-Summary-specific sections that turned out to reuse Problems'/Medications' own entry-level logic once actually researched: **Hospital Discharge Diagnosis** → `Condition` (`category="encounter-diagnosis"`) and **Discharge Medications** → `MedicationRequest`. Discharge Summary's own two still-deferred required sections (Hospital Course, Plan of Treatment) and History and Physical's own IG-required narrative sections (Reason for Visit, History of Present Illness, Physical Exam, Assessment, Plan of Care, Review of Systems, Social History, Family History, General Status) either use entry shapes this app doesn't recognize yet or are narrative-only, and are silently skipped - see the C-CDA subsection for the full disclosed scope limit. Input format is auto-detected (`app/pipeline.py`) so the same **Convert to FHIR**/**Validate**/**Generate sample** UI and `/api/convert`/`/api/validate`/`/api/generate` endpoints handle any of the three formats - no separate route/UI. See the C-CDA subsection below for the full shape.
- **X12 EDI → FHIR** (`app/edi/`) - the app's second non-HL7v2 *input format* (delimited text with self-describing delimiters, not pipe-delimited or XML). Phase 1: **270** (Eligibility Inquiry) → `CoverageEligibilityRequest`, **271** (Eligibility Response) → `CoverageEligibilityResponse`, both alongside a `Coverage` + payer `Organization` + subscriber (and optional dependent) `Patient` + provider `Organization`/`Practitioner`. Phase 2: **276** (Claim Status Request) / **277** (Claim Status Response) → `Task` (base FHIR R4 has no purpose-built claim-status-inquiry resource - see the EDI subsection below), one `Task` per claim-status entry, alongside payer/receiver/provider `Organization`/`Practitioner` and subscriber (and optional dependent) `Patient`. Phase 3: **278** (Health Care Services Review - Prior Authorization) → `Claim` (`use="preauthorization"`) always, plus a `ClaimResponse` referencing that same `Claim` when the message is a certification decision - **the one EDI pair sharing a single `ST01="278"`** for both directions (distinguished by `BHT02`, not `ST01`, unlike every other EDI family here), verified against real X12.org-published examples and, for the target FHIR shape specifically, against the official Da Vinci PAS IG. Phase 4: **835** (Health Care Claim Payment/Advice - Electronic Remittance Advice) → `PaymentReconciliation`, one `.detail[]` entry per claim payment - **the one EDI family with no `HL` hierarchy and no `BHT` segment at all**, a genuine structural break from every other family here (confirmed directly, not assumed). Phase 5: **837P** (Health Care Claim - Professional) → `Claim` (`use="claim"`, `type="professional"`) - the deepest, most cross-referential loop shape of any EDI phase here (a claim-level `HI` diagnosis list cross-referenced by position from each service line's own diagnosis-pointer composite). Phase 6: **837I** (Health Care Claim - Institutional) → `Claim` (`use="claim"`, `type="institutional"`) - **the one EDI trio in this app sharing a single `ST01="837"` with no `BHT02`-style field inside the transaction set to distinguish the variants** (unlike 278's own request/response split), disambiguated instead by `ST03` (verified as the officially authoritative field for exactly this - see the EDI subsection). Phase 7: **837D** (Health Care Claim - Dental) → `Claim` (`use="claim"`, `type="oral"`) - the third member of that same `ST01="837"` trio (also disambiguated by `ST03`), with its own genuinely different service-line segment (`SV3`, CDT procedure codes) and a dedicated `TOO` (Tooth Information) segment mapping tooth number/surfaces to real ADA canonical systems - completing the full "big five" HIPAA X12 transaction-set suite this app set out to cover. Same auto-detected, third branch of the format sniff in `app/pipeline.py` - no separate route/UI. See the EDI subsection below for the full shape and its disclosed verification-source gap (no official, free X12-to-FHIR ConceptMap IG exists in general, unlike HL7v2's v2-to-FHIR or C-CDA's C-CDA on FHIR - 278 is a real, disclosed exception to that gap via Da Vinci PAS's target-shape verification, even though its own X12-segment crosswalk still isn't published).
- **Bundle deduplication** (`app/dedup.py`) - README's own third named pillar, and the first one to operate on the *output* Bundle rather than a source message. An opt-in (never automatic) post-conversion pass, `deduplicate_bundle(bundle) -> DedupResult`, that merges duplicate `Patient`/`Practitioner`/`Organization`/`Location` entries within one already-converted Bundle - the four "identity" resource types other resources *reference*, deliberately excluding every clinical/event resource type (two identical-looking `Observation`s aren't necessarily the same real occurrence). Matches by `identifier` first (any shared `(system, value)` pair), falling back to a name-only key (`Patient`/`Practitioner`'s `HumanName` family+given, `Organization`/`Location`'s bare string `.name`) only when no identifier is present on either side; a resource with neither is never merged. A generic, resource-type-agnostic recursive walk (`_rewrite_references`, over `pydantic.BaseModel`/`list` structurally, not a maintained per-field list) rewrites every surviving resource's own `Reference.reference` that pointed at a removed duplicate to the kept, canonical resource's `urn:uuid` instead - reaches deeply-nested references (`Claim.careTeam[].provider`, `Encounter.participant[].individual`, ...) the same way regardless of which field they're on. **A real, evidenced motivating case, not a hypothetical one**: X12 837P/837I commonly carry a Billing Provider (2010AA) and a Rendering/Attending Provider (2310B) under the identical NPI - the common real-world shape for a solo practitioner billing under their own name - and this app's own existing `tests/fixtures/edi_837p_basic.x12` (adapted from the official X12.org example) already exercises exactly this, currently producing two separate `Practitioner` resources for one real person. Wired as a UI checkbox ("Deduplicate resources", next to Convert to FHIR only - it has no meaning for Validate, which runs against the source message, not a Bundle) and a `deduplicate: bool = False` field on `ConvertApiRequest`/`POST /api/convert` (JSON response gains a `deduplication: {resources_merged, merges}` key only when the flag was set) and the no-JS `POST /convert` form fallback. Never mutates the caller's input Bundle - `deduplicate_bundle` returns a fresh, deep-copied one.
- **Bidirectional transformation** (`app/transform/`) - README's own fifth named pillar, and the first capability in this app that produces raw source-format text as *output* rather than FHIR. A new top-level package sibling to `app/mappings/`/`app/cda/`/`app/edi/`, structured the same "different shape dispatched via registry" way (`app/transform/base.py::MessageBuilder(build_message(bundle) -> str)`, `app/transform/registry.py`'s flat `{(target_format, target_type, target_trigger): builder}` dict - trigger `""` for a target shape with no real trigger-event concept, mirroring `app/generators/registry.py`'s own `("CDA", "CCD")` precedent - `app/transform/pipeline.py::build_message_from_bundle(bundle, target_format, target_type, target_trigger)` as the single dispatch entry point). Unlike `app/pipeline.py`'s forward-direction dispatch, there's no format to *sniff*: a FHIR Bundle alone doesn't uniquely determine which target message type/format it should become (a Patient+Encounter Bundle could become an ADT A01/A02/.../A08, a CCD header, or an EDI 270 subscriber loop), so the caller names the target explicitly rather than the dispatcher inferring it. `app/transform/common.py` holds shared cross-target helpers (`find_resource`/`find_resources` - "the first entry of this resourceType," safe because this app's own forward pipelines never emit two Patients in one Bundle; `format_hl7_date`/`format_hl7_ts` - the reverse of `app/fhir_models/builders.py::parse_hl7_date`/`parse_hl7_datetime`, preserving a real timezone offset the same deliberate way that module does) - built proactively for a foreseeable second/third consumer (a future C-CDA/EDI reverse builder) rather than only after a literal second consumer forced the extraction, the same judgment call already made once for `app.edi.common.NM1_ID_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM`.
  - **`app/transform/hl7_adt.py`** — `AdtA01Builder`, the first slice, chosen for the identical reason ADT^A01 was this app's very first *forward*-direction slice: the simplest, best-understood shape (Patient + Encounter only), proving the reverse-direction architecture before a harder shape's own field-mapping questions have to be solved too. Reverses `app/mappings/common.py::build_patient`/`app/mappings/adt.py::build_encounter_core` field-for-field using each one's own exact field positions/component shapes (PID-3/5/7/8/11/13, PV1-2/3/7/19/44/45, EVN-2), not re-derived independently, so a future change to the forward mapping's field shape has an obvious reverse counterpart to update. **A real, disclosed round-trip fidelity gap, not a bug**: several HL7v2 fields the forward mapper reads have no FHIR-side home at all (MSH-3/4/5/6 sending/receiving application-facility) and get a fixed, disclosed placeholder value on the way back out (matching this app's own generator's "always produce valid output" precedent, since MSH's own fields are HL7v2-required); two forward-mapped fields are collapsed into a single *display string* rather than kept as separate components (`location_display` for PV1-3/6, `person_display` for PV1-7) - reversing a display string back into structured components is inherently lossy/ambiguous, so this builder makes one deliberate choice both fields share: the whole display string goes in the field's first component, the rest stays empty, rather than guessing at a split point. Verified as a genuine round trip, not just string formatting: `tests/test_transform.py::test_round_trip_produces_a_convertible_message_again` feeds the generated text back through the real forward pipeline and confirms the resulting Bundle's Patient/Encounter fields match. **`AdtA02Builder`/`AdtA03Builder`/`AdtA04Builder`/`AdtA05Builder`/`AdtA08Builder` shipped as a follow-up breadth pass** - renamed from `hl7_adt_a01.py` to `hl7_adt.py` (mirroring `app/mappings/adt.py`'s own single-file-per-message-type shape) once these five became real second/third/... slices of the identical Patient+Encounter shape, following this project's own established "add a subclass in that type's module rather than a new file" rule for a new trigger event, applied to the reverse direction for the first time. **A genuine simplification found once these were actually worked out, not assumed up front**: `_build_pv1` turned out to need no trigger-aware branching at all - it faithfully reflects whatever the source `Encounter` resource contains (PV1-6 prior location whenever a `status="completed"` location entry is present, distinguishing it from the `status="active"` current-location entry `AdtA02Mapper`'s own forward code sets; PV1-36 discharge disposition whenever `.hospitalization` is present) rather than needing to know which trigger is being built, since none of A02/A04/A05/A08's own forward-direction differences depend on a FHIR field the reverse direction wouldn't already be populating anyway - A08's own status inference in particular is automatically consistent on the way back out with zero A08-specific code, confirmed directly against both the finished and in-progress A08 fixtures. **`AdtA03Builder` is the one genuine exception**: it overrides `_validate` to raise `MappingError` when the source `Encounter` has no `period.end`, mirroring the forward `AdtA03Mapper`'s own identical requirement - the same "require the field this builder structurally depends on" precedent this app's forward direction already established. Verified via a 600-seed fuzz sweep (100 seeds × 6 triggers) of `generate("ADT", trigger, seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. **`AdtA11Builder`/`AdtA13Builder`/`AdtA38Builder` (the cancel-trigger siblings) shipped as a second follow-up breadth pass, confirmed - not assumed - to need zero cancel-specific reverse logic**: the forward mapper's own `_drop_evn2_period_start_fallback` hazard (EVN-2 on a cancel-trigger message is the cancel-notification time, not a real admission start, so the forward mapper ignores EVN-2 for `period.start` on every cancel trigger regardless of what PV1-44 says) means whatever this builder writes to EVN-2 is inert on the next forward pass - re-parsing a cancel-trigger message always re-derives `period.start` from PV1-44 alone, so a plain `trigger_event`-only subclass round-trips correctly with no EVN-2 special-casing needed on the way back out; `AdtA13Builder`'s own discharge-disposition round-trip likewise needs no trigger-specific code, since `_build_pv1` already writes `encounter.hospitalization.dischargeDisposition` to PV1-36 whenever it's present, trigger-agnostically, exactly the way A03's own discharge disposition already round-trips. Verified via a parametrized round trip against all three triggers' own real fixtures (`test_adt_cancel_trigger_round_trips_and_preserves_entered_in_error_status`, each confirming `status="entered-in-error"` and `period.start` survive exactly) plus `test_adt_a13_round_trips_discharge_disposition` and a 600-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 3 triggers) of `generate("ADT", trigger, seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward with zero failures - every HL7v2 trigger event this app converts *to* FHIR across all four message types (ADT/SIU/ORU/MDM) is now also a reverse-transform target. PID and MSH construction were promoted to `app/transform/hl7_common.py` once `app/transform/hl7_siu.py` became a second real consumer of the identical PID reversal - the app/transform/ mirror of `app/mappings/common.py::build_patient`'s own "PID is mapped identically regardless of message type" role.
  - **`app/transform/hl7_siu.py`** — `SiuS12Builder`, registered as `("HL7", "SIU", "S12")`. The fifth slice, and the first proof this architecture handles a genuinely different FHIR shape (`Appointment`, not `Encounter`) - scoped to S12 (new booking) alone, the same "one thing per slice" discipline every earlier reverse slice already established. Reverses `app/mappings/siu.py::build_appointment_core`/`_build_participants` field-for-field: `SCH-1`/`-2` (placer/filler identifiers), `SCH-7`/`-8` (reason/appointment type, CWE - reversed via the now-public `app.fhir_models.builders.CWE_FALLBACK_SYSTEM`, promoted once this became its second real consumer, to know when a coding's system should reverse back to an empty CWE component 3 vs. a real system string), `TQ1-6`/`-7`/`-8`, `AIS-3` (service type, CWE), `NTE-3` (comment, split back into one segment per original line - the reverse of `build_appointment_core`'s own `"\n".join`), and one `AIP`/`AIL`/`AIG` segment per non-patient `Appointment.participant`, dispatched by the referenced resource's own FHIR type. **A cross-cutting resolution this slice needed that ADT's own reverse builders didn't**: `Appointment.participant[]` can reference `Location` from *two* different forward-side sources - `AIL` (via `build_location_from_pl`, which sets only `.name`, no `.identifier` at all) and `AIG` when `AIG-4`'s resource-type code is location-like (via `_build_aig_resource`, which *does* set a `urn:interop-tools:location-id`-systemed `.identifier`) - this asymmetry, not a stored marker, is exactly how this builder tells the two apart on the way back out. Verified via a field-by-field comparison against the real `siu_s12_basic.hl7` fixture (status/start/end/duration/reasonCode/appointmentType/serviceType/comment all match exactly) and a 200-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("SIU", "S12", seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. **Disclosed round-trip fidelity gaps, the same category as every earlier slice's own**: `AIL-3`/`AIG-3` only round-trip their first component(s) (the same `location_display` collapse ADT's own PV1-3/6 already disclose), and a reversed `Practitioner` only recovers `id`/`family`/`given` (XCN components 1/2/3), not the trailing `^^^^MD` degree/suffix. `RGS` is not emitted at all - this app's own forward parser never requires it (confirmed directly: `SIU`'s `to_bundle()` reads `AIS`/`AIG`/`AIL`/`AIP` via `optional_segments()` with no `RGS`-grouping requirement). The CWE-reversal logic itself (originally module-private `_reverse_cwe` here) was promoted a second time, to `app/transform/hl7_common.py::reverse_cwe`, once `app/transform/hl7_oru.py` became a third real consumer of the identical reversal. **`SiuS13Builder`/`SiuS14Builder`/`SiuS15Builder`/`SiuS17Builder`/`SiuS26Builder` shipped as a follow-up breadth pass**, mirroring `app/mappings/siu.py`'s own `_BookedSiuMapper`/`_UntimedSiuMapper` split exactly rather than the bare `trigger_event`-only subclass swap ORU's/MDM's own breadth passes needed - the one HL7v2 breadth pass with a real, disclosed per-trigger behavioral difference, because SIU is the one message type here whose forward mappers themselves split the same way: `_BookedSiuBuilder` (S12/S13/S14/S26) overrides `_validate` to raise `MappingError` without a resolvable `Appointment.start`, the same "require the field this builder structurally depends on" precedent `hl7_adt.py`'s own `AdtA03Builder` already established, since the forward `_BookedSiuMapper` itself raises without one; `_BaseSiuBuilder`'s own no-op `_validate` covers S15/S17's genuinely untimed shape directly. Every other field this builder emits (SCH/TQ1/NTE/AIS/AIP/AIL/AIG) is trigger-agnostic - none of S13/S14/S15/S17/S26's own forward-direction differences from S12 depend on a FHIR field the reverse direction wouldn't already be populating anyway. Verified via a parametrized round trip against all five triggers' own real fixtures (`test_siu_trigger_round_trips_and_preserves_status`, each asserting the correct `MSH-9` trigger code and that `Appointment.status` survives a full round trip - `booked`/`booked`/`cancelled`/`entered-in-error`/`noshow` respectively) plus `test_siu_s26_missing_start_time_raises_mapping_error` and a 1200-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 6 triggers) of `generate("SIU", trigger, seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward with zero failures.
  - **`app/transform/hl7_oru.py`** — `OruR01Builder`, registered as `("HL7", "ORU", "R01")`. The sixth slice, and the second proof (after SIU's `Appointment`) that this architecture handles a genuinely different FHIR shape - `DiagnosticReport` + `Observation`, with a real *grouping* relationship (`DiagnosticReport.result[]` referencing only its own `Observation`s) neither ADT's `Encounter` nor SIU's flat participant list needed to reconstruct. Reverses `app/mappings/oru.py::build_observation`/`build_diagnostic_report` field-for-field: `OBR-4`/`-7`/`-8`/`-22`/`-25` and one `OBX` per `Observation` referenced by that report's own `.result[]` - not a flat scan of every `Observation` in the Bundle, mirroring `group_segments_by_leader`'s own forward-direction grouping guarantee. **A real, disclosed round-trip fidelity gap specific to this slice**: `Observation.status`'s reverse can't always recover the exact original `OBX-11` code, since the forward `_RESULT_STATUS_MAP` maps two distinct codes (`"D"`/`"W"`) to the identical FHIR `"entered-in-error"` status - the reverse table picks one disclosed representative (`"D"`) rather than guessing, the same "can't always recover a many-to-one forward mapping's original input" limitation `edi_271.py`'s own `.disposition` gap already discloses for a different field. `OBR-2`/`-3` (placer/filler numbers) have no FHIR-side home at all (the forward mapper never reads `DiagnosticReport.identifier`) and are left empty. Verified via a field-by-field comparison against the real `oru_r01_basic.hl7` fixture (both reports' status/result-count and every observation's value/status/interpretation/performer all match exactly) and a 200-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("ORU", "R01", seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. **`OruR30Builder`/`OruR31Builder`/`OruR32Builder`/`OruR40Builder` shipped as an immediate follow-up breadth pass**, confirmed genuinely trivial rather than just assumed: `app/mappings/oru.py::BaseOruMapper` handles all five triggers with byte-for-byte identical forward logic, so `_BaseOruBuilder` + five one-line `trigger_event` subclasses (the same shape `hl7_adt.py`'s own `_BaseAdtBuilder` breadth pass already established) was the whole change - no new field-mapping logic needed. Verified via the real `oru_r30_basic.hl7`/`_r31_basic.hl7`/`_r32_basic.hl7`/`_r40_basic.hl7` fixtures (each report's code/result-count preserved across a round trip) and a 1000-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 5 triggers) of `generate("ORU", trigger, seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures.
  - **`app/transform/hl7_mdm.py`** — `MdmT02Builder`, registered as `("HL7", "MDM", "T02")`. The seventh slice, and the third proof (after SIU's `Appointment`, ORU's `DiagnosticReport`+`Observation`) that this architecture handles a genuinely different FHIR shape - `DocumentReference` + a separately-referenced `Binary` carrying the document's own text body, neither of which any earlier reverse slice needed to reconstruct. Reverses `app/mappings/mdm.py::build_document_reference`/`_build_binary_from_obx` field-for-field: TXA-2/-3/-6/-9/-10/-12/-16/-18 (type/content-presentation/origination-date/originator/authenticator/master-identifier/file-name/confidentiality, CWE + XCN-practitioner reversal both reused from `hl7_common.py`), and one `OBX` per line of the referenced `Binary`'s own decoded text body - not a guess at how many `OBX` segments originally existed, since the forward mapper itself collapses that count down to one joined string with no count preserved anywhere in FHIR. `PV1` is rebuilt via the newly-shared `app.transform.hl7_common.build_minimal_pv1` (promoted from `hl7_oru.py`'s own private `_build_pv1` once this became its third real consumer of the identical "PV1-2 class + PV1-19 visit id only" reversal `app.mappings.common.build_minimal_encounter` itself reads on the forward side - `CLASS_TO_PATIENT_CLASS` was promoted alongside it from `hl7_adt.py`'s own private copy). **Two real, disclosed round-trip fidelity gaps specific to this slice**: TXA-19 (Document Availability Status) can't be recovered at all - the forward `_resolve_status` unconditionally returns `"current"` regardless of what TXA-19 actually said, so there's no signal on the FHIR side to reverse from; this builder always regenerates the one verified value, `"AV"`, the same "can't recover a many-to-one forward mapping's original input, pick the disclosed representative" precedent `hl7_oru.py`'s own OBX-11 gap already established. TXA-3's own reverse has the identical shape: `"TEXT"` and `"FORMATTED"` both map forward to the same `text/plain` MIME type, so a `text/plain` attachment always reverses to the disclosed representative `"TEXT"`. Verified via a field-by-field comparison against the real `mdm_t02_basic.hl7` fixture (type/master-identifier/date/description/security-label and both originator/authenticator Practitioners' own names all match exactly) plus the `mdm_t02_same_author_authenticator.hl7` fixture (the single deduplicated Practitioner survives a full round trip, still referenced by both `author`/`authenticator`) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("MDM", "T02", seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. **`MdmT04Builder`/`MdmT06Builder`/`MdmT08Builder`/`MdmT10Builder`/`MdmT11Builder` shipped as an immediate follow-up breadth pass**, confirmed genuinely trivial rather than just assumed: `app/mappings/mdm.py::BaseMdmMapper` handles all six triggers with byte-for-byte identical forward logic, so `_BaseMdmBuilder` + six one-line `trigger_event` subclasses (the same shape `hl7_oru.py`'s own R30/R31/R32/R40 breadth pass already established) was the whole change. No dedicated per-trigger fixture exists for T04/T06/T08/T10/T11 (unlike ORU's own R30/R31/R32/R40, which each got a real fixture) - verified instead via a parametrized round trip against real `generate("MDM", trigger, seed=7)` output for each, plus a 1200-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 6 triggers) of `generate("MDM", trigger, seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward with zero failures.
  - **`app/transform/cda_ccd.py`** — `CcdReverseBuilder`, registered as `("CDA", "CCD", "")` (empty trigger - CCD has no real trigger-event concept, mirroring `app/generators/registry.py`'s own `("CDA", "CCD")` precedent one level further into the reverse-registry key). The second slice, and the first proof this architecture generalizes across input *formats*, not just across HL7v2 trigger events - scoped to exactly "header + Problems section," the identical scope CCD's own very first *forward*-direction slice shipped with (confirmed via git history: "Add C-CDA to FHIR conversion (CCD header + Problems section)" was this app's first CDA commit). Reverses `app/cda/common.py::build_patient_from_header`/`build_encounter_from_header` and `app/cda/problems.py::build_conditions` field-for-field. `_reverse_identifier_root(identifier)` is the one piece of real cross-cutting logic this slice needed that ADT^A01 didn't: an `Identifier.system` of `urn:oid:<root>` (the shape `app.cda.common.build_identifier` itself always produces when the source `<id>` had a real `@root`) reverses cleanly by stripping the prefix back off, verified directly against the real `ccd_basic.xml` fixture's own `urn:oid:2.16.840.1.113883.19.5` patient identifier round-tripping byte-for-byte - falling back to a disclosed placeholder OID only for a `system` with nothing recoverable (this app's own `urn:interop-tools:...` fallback systems, or an arbitrary non-OID string). **The same category of disclosed round-trip simplification as ADT^A01's display-string fields**: `Condition.clinicalStatus` is written back only via the Concern Act's own `statusCode` (the reverse of `_ACT_STATUS_TO_CLINICAL_STATUS`) - the nested Status Observation path is a legitimate alternate source on the forward side but isn't reconstructed here, since a FHIR clinical-status code alone doesn't indicate which of the two source shapes originally produced it, and the Concern Act's own `statusCode` is a first-class source in its own right, not a fabricated fallback. **The Medications section shipped as a follow-up breadth pass**, the first proof this builder's own scope generalizes past "header + Problems" the same way CCD's own *forward* direction grew past its first slice - reverses `app/cda/medications.py::build_medication_request`/`_build_dosage` field-for-field: `statusCode`/`moodCode` (reversed via `_MEDICATION_STATUS_TO_ACT_STATUS`/`_INTENT_TO_MOOD_CODE`, each picking a disclosed representative for a FHIR value the forward side's own many-to-one fallback can't uniquely recover - `"active"`/`"INT"` respectively, matching the forward side's own `_DEFAULT_STATUS`/`_DEFAULT_INTENT` defaults), `routeCode`/`doseQuantity`/`rateQuantity`/`effectiveTime` bounds (structured dosing), the free-text SIG `entryRelationship` (patientInstruction), and `consumable/manufacturedProduct/manufacturedMaterial/code`. **A real, pre-existing round-trip fidelity bug was caught and fixed while building this, not just deduplicated away**: `_build_cd_attrs(coding)` - the shared code/codeSystem/displayName attribute builder now used by both Problems' `<value xsi:type="CD">` and Medications' `<code>`/`<routeCode>` - was promoted from Problems' own inlined value-building, which had been writing `coding.system` (a FHIR system URL, e.g. `http://snomed.info/sct`) directly into the CDA `codeSystem` attribute instead of reversing it back to an OID via the newly-public `app.cda.common.OID_TO_FHIR_SYSTEM` (inverted, not re-derived, as `_FHIR_SYSTEM_TO_OID`) - so a second reverse-then-forward cycle silently corrupted `Condition.code.coding[0].system` into `urn:oid:http://snomed.info/sct`. Reproduced directly against `ccd_basic.xml` before the fix and confirmed fixed after - see `tests/test_transform.py::test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_coding_system_not_just_code`. `_reverse_code_system` also fixes the *absent*-codeSystem case honestly: a FHIR system of `app.cda.common.CD_FALLBACK_SYSTEM` (the forward side's own marker for "no `codeSystem` attribute was present at all") reverses to no `codeSystem` attribute, not a fabricated placeholder OID - only a genuinely unrecognized non-OID, non-fallback FHIR system falls back to a disclosed placeholder. Verified via a field-by-field comparison against the real `ccd_medications_basic.xml` fixture (status/intent/medication code+system/route/dose/period/patientInstruction all match exactly across both its structured-dosing and free-text-SIG entries) plus `ccd_medications_negated.xml` (a negated entry produces no `MedicationRequest` on the forward side, so the reverse builder never sees one and correctly emits no Medications section at all) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_ccd()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. **The Allergies section shipped as a second follow-up breadth pass**, the deepest CCD section reversed so far - it needed to reconstruct three levels of nested Status/Criticality/Reaction(+Severity) Observations, not just one flat entry shape - reverses `app/cda/allergies.py::_build_allergy_intolerance`/`_resolve_clinical_status`/`_resolve_criticality`/`_build_reactions` field-for-field: the allergen itself via `participant[@typeCode=CSM]/participantRole/playingEntity/code` (reusing `_build_cd_attrs`, its second real consumer beyond Problems'/Medications' own coded fields), `clinicalStatus` via a real nested Status Observation this builder actually reconstructs (unlike Problems' own deliberate simplification of writing back only the Concern Act's `statusCode` - Allergies has no such alternate source to fall back to, since the IG marks the Concern Act's own `statusCode`/`effectiveTime` "not supported" for this section, so skipping the Status Observation here would silently lose every non-default `clinicalStatus` value on a round trip), `criticality`/reaction `severity` via their own nested Observations (`_CRITICALITY_TO_HL7_CODE`/`_SEVERITY_TO_SNOMED`, both clean bijections of `CRITICALITY_MAP`/`SEVERITY_MAP`), and `type`/`category` via `_reverse_allergy_value_code` - a three-pass best-match search (exact (type, category), then type-only, then category-only) over `TYPE_MAP`/`CATEGORY_MAP` combined, since both are keyed off the identical source SNOMED code and a single source code can carry a type, a category, or both (both tables promoted to public in `app/cda/allergies.py`, the same "promote once a second real consumer exists" discipline as `OID_TO_FHIR_SYSTEM`). **A real, disclosed negation-detection mechanism, not a guess**: a negated `AllergyIntolerance` always reaches this builder with `code.coding` empty (only `code.text` set) - `_resolve_allergen_code` never returns a coded `CodeableConcept` for a negated entry - so `not (allergy.code and allergy.code.coding)` is a reliable signal for `negationInd="true"` with no dedicated FHIR-side marker needed. **A further, disclosed lossy step follows from this, not a new one**: a negated allergy that still carried a resolvable allergen (`"No known allergy to Penicillin"`) can't have that allergen's own code recovered here (the forward side only kept a display string inside free text, not the coding), so it degrades to the fully generic `"No known allergies"` shape on this and every subsequent round trip - the same "can't recover more than the forward side actually kept" limitation every other lossy-by-construction reverse mapping in this app already discloses, confirmed to at least round-trip *exactly* for the fully-unresolvable-allergen case that IS fully recoverable (`ccd_allergies_no_known.xml`). Verified via a field-by-field comparison against the real `ccd_allergies_basic.xml` fixture (allergen code+system, type, category, clinicalStatus, criticality, onset, recordedDate, and the reaction's own manifestation+severity all match exactly - the deepest single-fixture assertion coverage of any CCD reverse slice so far) plus `ccd_allergies_no_known.xml`'s own exact-round-trip case and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_ccd()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. **The Immunizations section shipped as a third follow-up breadth pass**, structurally simpler than Allergies (no wrapping Act, no nested Status/Criticality Observations - the identical bare-`substanceAdministration` shape Medications already established) but with its own genuinely different status-reversal shape: reverses `app/cda/immunizations.py::_build_immunization`/`_resolve_status` field-for-field - `vaccineCode`/`routeCode`/`doseQuantity`/`lotNumberText` (the first two via `_build_cd_attrs`, its third real consumer), and `occurrenceDateTime` via a bare `<effectiveTime value="...">` (`occurrenceString == "Unknown"`, the forward side's own disclosed no-date fallback, is deliberately never re-encoded - omitting `<effectiveTime>` entirely lets the next forward pass regenerate the identical `"Unknown"` fallback naturally rather than fabricating a fake timestamp). **A real, deliberate reversal-direction choice for `STATUS_MAP`'s own many-to-one shape, disclosed rather than left to accidental dict-comprehension key-ordering**: `Immunization.status == "not-done"` can come from any of six distinct forward `statusCode` values *or* `negationInd="true"` unconditionally - reversed via `negationInd="true"` specifically (not one of the six statusCode values picked arbitrarily), since the forward side's own comment already treats negation as the dominant real-world signal for this status ("negation is checked first, before this table is even consulted"); a real, inert `statusCode="aborted"` is still emitted alongside it purely for XML realism, since negation short-circuits status resolution before `statusCode` is ever consulted on the next forward pass regardless of what it says. Verified via a field-by-field comparison against the real `ccd_immunizations_basic.xml` fixture (status/vaccine code+system/lot number/route/dose all match exactly across both its administered and negated-refusal entries, with the fixture's third, `INT`-mood planned entry correctly never appearing on either side) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_ccd()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. **The Vital Signs section shipped as a fourth follow-up breadth pass, and the second proof (after ORU's `DiagnosticReport`) that this app's reverse direction can reconstruct a real grouping relationship, not just a flat entry list**: reverses `app/cda/vitals.py::build_vital_signs` field-for-field - one Vital Signs Organizer per panel `Observation`, wrapping one `<component><observation>` per member `Observation` its own `.hasMember` references, plus `code`/`effectiveTime`/`value`/`interpretationCode`/`methodCode`/`targetSiteCode` on each member (the first three of those via `_build_cd_attrs`, its fourth real consumer). **A genuinely new resolution problem this slice needed, distinct from ORU's own report/observation grouping**: nothing marks *which* `Observation`s in a flat `Bundle.entry` list are Vital Signs panels versus individual members, since both are the same resource type (unlike ORU, where `DiagnosticReport` and `Observation` are different types) - `_is_vital_signs_panel(observation)` resolves this the same "check the real, disclosed marker, not a structural guess" way `hl7_siu.py`'s own AIL-vs-AIG Location disambiguation already does: the forward mapper's own fixed LOINC `85353-1` "Vital Signs Panel" code (now `app.cda.vitals.PANEL_CODE`, promoted to public) is the one reliable signal, checked directly rather than inferred from `.hasMember` presence alone. `statusCode`/organizer `<code>` are both fixed per the C-CDA spec itself on the forward side (not just this app's own disclosed default), so the reverse builder emits the identical fixed values unconditionally, with no ConceptMap to build - the one CCD section reversed so far with literally no status-mapping ambiguity at all. **Disclosed scope limit inherited directly from the forward side, not a new one**: the IG's own Blood Pressure/Pulse Oximetry component-grouping special cases were never built on the forward side either, so there's nothing for this reverse slice to reconstruct there - every vital sign still round-trips 1:1, just not in the profile-conformant grouped shape those two special cases would produce. Verified via a field-by-field comparison against the real `ccd_vitals_basic.xml` fixture (the panel's own two-member `.hasMember` count, and each member's code/value/unit/interpretation, all match exactly) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_ccd()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. **The Results section shipped as a fifth follow-up breadth pass**, the same panel/member grouping shape as Vital Signs but wrapped in `DiagnosticReport` instead of a panel `Observation` - reverses `app/cda/results.py::_build_result_observation`/`_build_observation_value`/`_build_reference_range` field-for-field: `code`/`effectiveTime`/`interpretationCode`/`methodCode`/`targetSiteCode`/`referenceRange` (`IVL_PQ` low/high) on each member `Observation`, plus the report's own `code`/`effectiveTime`. **No panel-vs-member disambiguation problem this time, unlike Vital Signs**: `DiagnosticReport` and `Observation` are already different FHIR resource types, so `report.result[]` unambiguously identifies which `Observation`s belong to which report - the identical "look up by referenced id, not by iterating a same-typed pool" pattern `hl7_oru.py`'s own report/observation grouping already established, reused here rather than re-derived. **A genuine non-issue confirmed directly, not a disclosed gap**: `_build_result_value_element` always emits `xsi:type="PQ"` for a `valueQuantity`, never `"REAL"`, even though the forward side can produce either - confirmed by reading `_build_observation_value` itself that both branches call the identical `build_quantity_from_pq` with no `REAL`-specific behavior, so which xsi:type this builder emits is provably inert for round-trip correctness, not a corner cut. `STATUS_MAP`'s own many-to-one shape (`"registered"` alone has three source candidates: active/held/suspended) is reversed via one disclosed representative per target value (`"active"`/`"cancelled"`/`"completed"` for `registered`/`cancelled`/`final` respectively - `"cancelled"` picks its own exact-match source code rather than a different one, since an unambiguous identity mapping exists for it unlike the other two), the same deliberate-disclosure discipline `hl7_mdm.py`'s own status reversal and `hl7_oru.py`'s own OBX-11 gap already established, rather than whatever a naive dict-comprehension inversion's key-ordering would pick arbitrarily. Verified via a field-by-field comparison against the real `ccd_results_basic.xml` fixture (report code/status/result-count, and both member observations' own values - one `PQ` with a `referenceRange`, one free-text `ST` - all match exactly, exercising two of `_build_observation_value`'s own xsi:type branches in one fixture) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_ccd()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. **The Procedures section shipped as a sixth follow-up breadth pass, completing every general-purpose section the forward direction recognizes on any CCD-shaped document** - a flat, single-`<procedure>`-per-entry shape (no organizer, no grouping) that turned out to need the *least* new machinery of any section reversed so far. Reverses `app/cda/procedures.py::_build_procedure`/`_resolve_status` field-for-field: `code`/`targetSiteCode` (via `_build_cd_attrs`, its fifth real consumer), `performedDateTime`/`performedPeriod` (mutually exclusive per the IG, mirroring `ivl_ts_bounds`' own point-in-time-vs-range distinction on the forward side), and `identifier`. **`STATUS_MAP` turned out to be a genuine bijection, confirmed by inspection rather than assumed** - unlike every other status map this app has reversed (ORU's OBX-11, Medications', Immunizations', Results'), each of Procedures' four target values (`stopped`/`in-progress`/`not-done`/`completed`) is distinct, so this is the first status reversal in this app inverted directly (`{v: k for k, v in STATUS_MAP.items()}`) rather than hand-picked. **The one real remaining ambiguity is deliberately, not accidentally, resolved**: `"not-done"` still has two forward sources outside the table itself - the table's own `"cancelled"` row, or `negationInd="true"` unconditionally overriding any `statusCode` - and this builder always regenerates the former (no `negationInd` attribute needed), a disclosed choice between two equally-valid, indistinguishable-on-the-FHIR-side origins, not a guess at which one a given resource "really" came from. `"unknown"` (the forward side's own fallback for an absent/unrecognized `statusCode`) reverses by omitting `<statusCode>` entirely, regenerating that identical fallback rather than fabricating a fake code - the same precedent Results' own `STATUS_MAP` reversal already established. **`_reverse_generic_identifier` is this app's first reverse consumer of `app.cda.common.build_identifier`'s full three-shape resolution** (root+extension, root-only, fallback-system-with-no-root) - every earlier reverse slice's own identifier handling (`_reverse_identifier_root`) only ever needed the simpler root+extension shape, since header-level ids always carry a real extension; Procedures' own real fixture happens to exercise the root-only shape directly (`ccd_procedures_basic.xml`'s negated Colonoscopy entry), making this the first slice that actually needed the fuller reversal. Verified via a field-by-field comparison against the real `ccd_procedures_basic.xml` fixture (status/code/performedDateTime-or-Period/bodySite/identifier all match exactly across both its completed-point-in-time and negated-period entries, including the root-only identifier shape) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_ccd()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures.
  - **`app/transform/cda_discharge_summary.py`** — `DischargeSummaryReverseBuilder` (`DISCHARGE_SUMMARY_TEMPLATE_ID = "2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.1.8"`): the second document type, and the first real proof that `build_sectioned_bundle`'s reverse-direction mirror generalizes to a second C-CDA document type - `build_message()` is, like `CcdReverseBuilder`'s own, just `return build_sectioned_document(bundle, DISCHARGE_SUMMARY_TEMPLATE_ID, "18842-5", "Discharge Summary", "Discharge Summary", include_discharge_specific_sections=True)`. `app.transform.cda_ccd.build_sectioned_document(bundle, template_id, doc_code, doc_code_display, title, include_discharge_specific_sections=False)` was extracted from `CcdReverseBuilder`'s own previously-inlined `build_message()` once this became a second real consumer of the identical header+all-seven-sections assembly - confirmed structurally identical to CCD's own header shape the same way the forward `DischargeSummaryBuilder` itself was (both share `recordTarget`/`componentOf`, verified against a real HL7 C-CDA-Examples Discharge Summary). **One of the two discharge-specific sections is now genuinely reversed, resolved as a follow-up once each was actually researched - the same "partial gap turned out to be fixable, the rest genuinely isn't" shape the forward direction's own Hospital Discharge Diagnosis/Discharge Medications slice already went through**: `include_discharge_specific_sections=True` makes `build_sectioned_document` split out any `Condition` carrying `category == "encounter-diagnosis"` (the real, reliable marker `app.cda.hospital_discharge_diagnosis`'s own forward module sets, promoted to public `CATEGORY_SYSTEM`/`CATEGORY_CODE` once this became its first real reverse-direction consumer - a plain Problems section never populates `.category` at all) into its own regenerated Hospital Discharge Diagnosis section, via `_build_problem_entry`'s newly-added `act_template_id` parameter (defaulting to Problems' own Concern Act, overridden here with the Hospital Discharge Diagnosis Act's templateId instead - both wrap the byte-for-byte identical Problem Observation entry per that forward module's own docstring, so only the outer Act templateId genuinely needs to change). **Discharge Medications remains a disclosed, permanent limitation, not a deferred slice, confirmed rather than assumed**: the forward `app.cda.discharge_medications` module reuses `build_medication_request` with zero modification, so a `MedicationRequest` sourced from that section is byte-for-byte structurally identical, on the FHIR side, to one sourced from a plain Medications section (confirmed by reading that module's own docstring) - there is no marker this builder could reverse even in principle, so every `MedicationRequest` continues to route into the plain Medications section regardless of which document type is being reversed; this is the most correct behavior achievable, not a placeholder. `CcdReverseBuilder`/`HistoryAndPhysicalReverseBuilder` both keep the default `include_discharge_specific_sections=False` - a `Condition` reversed through either one always folds into the plain Problems section, matching the forward direction's own scoping (`app.cda.registry.SECTION_BUILDERS` registers Hospital Discharge Diagnosis's own templateId at all, so this is a real if atypical possibility for any document type in principle, but this app's own CCD/H&P generators never produce one, confirmed via `test_ccd_round_trip_never_splits_out_hospital_discharge_diagnosis_section`). Verified via `discharge_summary_basic.xml`'s own full round trip (`test_discharge_summary_round_trip_splits_discharge_diagnosis_from_plain_problems`: the discharge-diagnosis Condition's own code appears inside the regenerated Hospital Discharge Diagnosis Act specifically, not the plain Concern Act, `category` survives exactly, and the Discharge Medications-sourced `MedicationRequest` still survives via the plain Medications section, `10183-2`'s own Discharge Medications LOINC code never regenerated) plus the pre-existing `test_discharge_summary_round_trip_produces_a_convertible_document_again` (Encounter class/period and every Condition's own code all match exactly, resource-type set unchanged) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_discharge_summary()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures, including a dedicated category-presence-set comparison per seed.
  - **`app/transform/cda_history_and_physical.py`** — `HistoryAndPhysicalReverseBuilder` (`HISTORY_AND_PHYSICAL_TEMPLATE_ID = "2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.1.3"`): the third document type, and a second real proof (after Discharge Summary) that `build_sectioned_document` generalizes - `build_message()` is, like the other two builders', just `return build_sectioned_document(bundle, HISTORY_AND_PHYSICAL_TEMPLATE_ID, "34117-2", "History and physical note", "History and Physical Note")`. Confirmed structurally identical to CCD/Discharge Summary at the header level (`recordTarget`, `componentOf/encompassingEncounter`) by fetching a real HL7 C-CDA-Examples History and Physical document and comparing directly - the same fetch that surfaced the Procedures "entries optional" gap fixed above. **A real, disclosed scope limit, not a redesign - mirroring Discharge Summary's own precedent exactly**: an H&P Note's own IG-required narrative sections (Reason for Visit/Chief Complaint, History of Present Illness, Physical Exam, Assessment, Plan of Care, Review of Systems, Social History, Family History, General Status) either carry no structured entries at all (narrative-only, would need a proper FHIR Composition, already out-of-scope) or use entry shapes this app's existing section builders don't recognize - none are mapped this slice, silently skipped the same way. Verified directly against the real fetched example (not assumed): alongside these H&P-specific sections, it also carried Allergies, Immunizations, Medications, Problems, Procedures, Results, and Vital Signs sections - **every one of this app's seven currently-recognized section types in a single document** - so a real H&P Note converts to something genuinely useful (header + all seven) even without the H&P-specific narrative ones. Unlike Discharge Summary (`force_encounter=True` in the generator, since a discharge is inherently tied to one hospitalization), an H&P's own `componentOf/encompassingEncounter` is genuinely optional in the generator too - a real H&P can precede any admission (e.g. a pre-operative visit), matching CCD's own optional-encounter treatment rather than Discharge Summary's forced one.
  - **UI/API wiring**: a second, independent form ("FHIR → Message") below the existing Convert/Validate one - a `bundle_json` textarea (with a "Use Bundle above" link-button that copies the most recent Convert output into it, since pasting a Bundle by hand is the friction point a real user would hit first) and a target-format `<select>` populated from `list_supported_targets()` (`HL7 ADT^A01` and `CDA CCD` today - more appear automatically as future builders are registered, no template change needed; a target with no trigger renders without a trailing `^`, e.g. `"CDA CCD"` not `"CDA CCD^"` - `str.partition` on a caret-less label still parses back to an empty `target_trigger` correctly, so this is a display fix, not a parsing-format change). `POST /api/transform` (`{bundle_json, target_format, target_type, target_trigger}` → `{message_text}`) and the no-JS `POST /transform` form fallback mirror `/api/convert`'s/`/convert`'s exact error-handling shape (`json.JSONDecodeError` → 400 "Invalid JSON", `pydantic.ValidationError` from `Bundle.model_validate` → 422 "Invalid FHIR Bundle", `MappingError` → 422 "Mapping error" - covering both "no builder registered for this target" and "the Bundle lacks a resource this builder structurally needs"). **A real, disclosed browser-rendering caveat, not a spec violation**: HL7v2's own `\r` segment terminator doesn't reliably render as a line break inside `<pre>`/`<code>` the way it does in a `<textarea>`, so both the JS-driven and server-rendered display paths substitute `\r`→`\n` for **display only** - the JSON API's own `message_text` field always returns the real `\r`-terminated text unchanged, and copying the displayed (substituted) text back into this app's own "Raw HL7v2 message" textarea round-trips correctly regardless, since `app/hl7/parser.py::parse_message()` already normalizes `\n`/`\r\n` to `\r` on the way in. **A real UI bug caught by testing in an actual browser (Puppeteer), not just via route-level tests**: the "Use Bundle above" button's initial `hidden` attribute is set once at server-render time from `result` - correct for the no-JS form-post fallback, but the default JS-driven fetch() flow never re-evaluates it after a successful Convert, so the button stayed permanently hidden in the primary, JS-enabled path. Fixed by having `showResult()`/`showError()`/`showValidationResult()` in `app/static/app.js` explicitly toggle `useBundleBtn.hidden` alongside the panes they already show/hide.
  - **`app/transform/edi_270.py`** — `Edi270Builder`, registered as `("EDI", "270", "")`. The third slice, and the first proof this architecture generalizes to a delimited-text format with its own self-describing envelope (`ISA`/`GS`/`ST`...`SE`/`GE`/`IEA`) and `HL`-hierarchy loop structure, not just to pipe-delimited HL7v2 and XML C-CDA - scoped to 270's own real Phase-1 forward scope (payer 2000A / provider 2000B / subscriber 2000C / optional dependent 2000D, one `EQ` item, one `DTP` serviced date; 270/271 had no earlier, narrower slice the way CCD's Problems-only or ADT's A01-only slices did, so this reverse builder's scope is 270's own full Phase-1 scope, not a further-reduced subset of it). Reverses `app/edi/common.py::build_organization_from_nm1`/`build_practitioner_from_nm1`/`build_patient_from_nm1_dmg`/`build_coverage`/`build_service_type_category` field-for-field, using the same `NM1_ID_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` table in reverse that `resolve_id_qualifier_system` builds forward. **Two genuinely new pieces of cross-cutting logic this slice needed that the other two didn't, both "resolve the real reference, don't guess from Bundle order" in spirit**: nothing on a bare FHIR `Patient` distinguishes "subscriber" from "dependent" - both are plain `Patient` resources - so `_resolve_subscriber_and_dependent` (now `app/transform/edi_common.py::resolve_subscriber_and_dependent`, see that module's own bullet below) reads `Coverage.subscriber`/`.beneficiary` (which this app's own `build_coverage()` always populates) to tell them apart, falling back to Bundle order only when no `Coverage` is present at all; similarly, `resolve_payer_and_provider` reads `CoverageEligibilityRequest.insurer`/`.provider` rather than assuming the payer always precedes the provider in `Bundle.entry` (which happens to be true given the forward mapper's own append order, but isn't a contract this module should rely on). Verified via a 200-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_270()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures, in addition to the standard fixture-based round-trip tests.
  - **`app/transform/edi_common.py`** — shared reverse-direction X12 helpers (`build_org_nm1`/`build_person_nm1`/`build_dmg`, `resolve_payer_and_provider`, `resolve_subscriber_and_dependent`, envelope/trailer assembly), extracted from `edi_270.py` once `edi_271.py` became a second real consumer of the identical envelope/HL/NM1/DMG construction - the same "extract on second real consumer" discipline this app's forward direction already applies dozens of times (e.g. `app/edi/common.py::build_coverage`). `resolve_payer_and_provider(bundle, insurer_reference, provider_reference)` takes the provider reference as an explicit parameter rather than hardcoding a field name, since **`CoverageEligibilityResponse` has no `.provider` field at all** (confirmed via `model_fields`, only `CoverageEligibilityRequest` does) - 271's own caller passes `provider_reference=None` and the function falls back straight to "the Organization that isn't the payer," while 270's caller passes the real reference.
  - **`app/transform/edi_271.py`** — `Edi271Builder`, registered as `("EDI", "271", "")`. The fourth slice, 270's own sibling, proving a second transaction set within the same EDI family reuses the shared envelope/loop infrastructure with only the patient-loop leader segment (`EB`, not `EQ`) and target-resource field reads genuinely diverging - mirroring the identical "genuinely different structure, don't force it through one shared helper" boundary `app/edi/eligibility_270.py`/`_271.py` themselves already draw on the forward side. Reverses `_build_insurance_items`/`_resolve_outcome_and_disposition` field-for-field: `EB01`/`EB03`/`EB05`/`EB12` from `.excluded`/`.category`/`.description`/`.network` (the now-public `app.edi.eligibility_271.EB12_NETWORK_TEXT` reversed, promoted from module-private once this became its second real consumer), and `AAA01`/`AAA03` from `.outcome`/`.disposition`. **One genuine, disclosed one-way gap**: `.disposition` is free text built forward from a `", ".join`-ed set of reject-reason codes with no delimiter contract a reverse parser could safely split back apart, so this builder emits one disclosed placeholder reject code (X12's own generic `"85"` "Dependent Ineligible") whenever `.outcome == "error"`, rather than attempting to un-parse the free text - `.outcome` itself round-trips exactly, `.disposition`'s own precise wording does not. Verified via the same 200-seed fuzz sweep discipline as 270 (0 failures across both 270 and 271, 400 seeds total).
  - **`app/transform/claim_status.py`** — `Edi276Builder`/`Edi277Builder`, registered as `("EDI", "276", "")`/`("EDI", "277", "")`. The eleventh slice, and the first proof this app's reverse direction generalizes to a **third** X12 family beyond the 270/271 sibling pair, with a genuinely deeper loop shape - a five-level HL chain (270/271's own is four) - and a target FHIR resource (`Task`) that can carry claim-status entries for *both* the subscriber and a dependent within one transaction set, mirroring the forward direction's own identical structural break from 270/271's single "one patient per transaction" rule. Reverses `app/edi/claim_status.py::_build_tasks_for_patient_loop`/`_build_status_concept`/`_resolve_task_status` field-for-field: one `TRN` (+ `STC` for 277 only) group per `Task`, from `Task.identifier` (trace number) and `Task.businessStatus`/`.status`. **Two genuinely new cross-cutting resolution problems, neither of which 270/271's own shared resolvers cover**: payer/provider are recovered directly from any `Task`'s own `.owner`/`.requester` references (every `Task` this app's forward mapper builds carries both) - a more direct resolution than 270/271's own Bundle-order fallback; the Information Receiver (2000B) has **no FHIR-side reference pointing to it at all** - resolved by exclusion instead, the remaining `Organization`/`Practitioner` that's neither the resolved payer nor provider, the same "the one that's left" spirit as `resolve_payer_and_provider`'s own provider fallback, applied here to a resource that's never directly referenced from anywhere on the FHIR side; subscriber vs. dependent `Patient` has no `Coverage` resource to disambiguate via (this family never builds one), so it's resolved via Bundle order instead - the identical disclosed fallback `resolve_subscriber_and_dependent` itself already falls back to when no `Coverage` is present. `STC_CATEGORY_PREFIX_TO_TASK_STATUS`'s own many-to-one shape (`"E"`/`"D"` both map to `Task.status="failed"`) is reversed via one disclosed representative category code per target status (`"E1"` for `"failed"`, an exact-match candidate over `"D0"`); `Task.businessStatus`'s own coding, when present, is read back directly for the real `STC01-1`/`STC01-2` pair rather than re-derived from `.status` alone, since re-deriving would lose precision within a category (e.g. the specific `"A3"` "Rejected" code, not just its `"A"` prefix). **Disclosed round-trip fidelity gaps**: `STC02` (status date) and every `REF`/`DTP` in the claim-status group have no FHIR-side home at all - the forward mapper's own `group_by_leader` walk captures them as group members but never reads any of them into a `Task` field - so none are regenerated here, the same "no source field, disclosed omission" precedent every earlier EDI reverse slice already established for its own unmapped `REF` segments. `STC_CATEGORY_SYSTEM`/`STC_STATUS_SYSTEM`/`TRACE_NUMBER_SYSTEM` were promoted to public in `app/edi/claim_status.py` for this reversal, the same "promote once a second real consumer exists" discipline as `OID_TO_FHIR_SYSTEM`. Verified via both real fixtures (276's own two `"requested"` tasks for subscriber+dependent both resolve correctly; 277's own two tasks' `businessStatus` round-trips exactly; the dedicated `edi_277_error_status.x12` fixture's `"E1"` category confirms the `"failed"` status round-trips too) and a 400-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 2 triggers) of `generate("EDI", trigger, seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures.
  - **`app/transform/prior_auth.py`** — `Edi278RequestBuilder`/`Edi278ResponseBuilder`, registered as `("EDI", "278REQUEST", "")`/`("EDI", "278RESPONSE", "")` (synthetic type strings, mirroring `app/edi/generator.py`'s own identical precedent for this family's generator entries, since 278 has no second `ST01` to key a second registry entry off of). The twelfth slice. Reverses `app/edi/prior_auth.py::_build_claim`/`_build_claim_response` field-for-field: `UM03` (service type, reusing `SERVICE_TYPE_CODE_SYSTEM`), `HI` (diagnosis composites - the first reverse consumer of this shape), `HCR01`/`HCR02`/`HCR03` for the response. **Payer/requester/subscriber/dependent resolution reuses 270/271's own shared resolvers directly, unlike `claim_status.py`'s own new ones**: 278 builds a real `Coverage` (unlike 276/277, which never does), so `resolve_subscriber_and_dependent` already has the signal it needs, and `Claim.insurer`/`.provider` are direct references, so `resolve_payer_and_provider` applies unchanged too - confirming this family's own shape is genuinely closer to 270/271's than to 276/277's, consistent with the forward module's own docstring ("2000A-2000D's HL03 codes are identical to 270/271's own table"). **A structural simplification specific to 278, confirmed from the forward mapper's own precedence rule**: whenever a dependent exists at all (recovered via `Coverage.beneficiary != .subscriber`), it is unconditionally also "the patient" (the one carrying the 2000E Patient Event loop) - so `dependent is not None` alone decides which loop gets the 2000E child, no extra check against `Claim.patient` needed. **Disclosed round-trip fidelity gaps**: `HI`'s own qualifier (`"ABK"` vs. `"ABF"` for ICD-10-CM, `"BK"` vs. `"BF"` for ICD-9-CM) can't be recovered from `Claim.diagnosis[].diagnosisCodeableConcept.coding[0].system` alone, since both qualifiers in a pair map to the identical FHIR system - reversed via the same positional convention every real 278 sender uses (position 1 = principal, every other position = other); `HCR01`/`HCR03` are read back directly from `ClaimResponseItemAdjudication`'s own real coding when present, falling back to a disclosed representative `HCR01` (`"A1"`, preferred over `"A3"` for the many-to-one `outcome="complete"` case) only when no adjudication exists at all. `HCR_ACTION_SYSTEM`/`HCR_REASON_SYSTEM` were promoted to public in `app/edi/prior_auth.py` for this reversal. **A real, pre-existing bug in all four 278 fixtures (and three inline test-built segments) was caught and fixed while building this, not just deduplicated away**: every person-type `NM1` segment (subscriber, dependent, person-typed requester) had one extra `*` before its ID-qualifier component, shifting `NM108`/`NM109` one position right so `_build_nm1_identifier` read the literal qualifier string (`"MI"`) as if it were the ID value, silently discarding the real member id (e.g. `"12345678901"`) - undetected by the existing test suite because no test asserted on a subscriber/dependent's own `Patient.identifier` value, only on `Coverage.subscriber.reference` (which resource it pointed to, not what identifier that resource carried). Confirmed isolated to hand-authored fixture/test text, not the generator (`app/edi/generator.py::build_person_nm1` was already correct) - fixed in all four `.x12` fixtures plus `tests/test_prior_auth_mapping.py`'s own inline segments, with a new regression assertion (`patient.identifier[0].value == "12345678901"`) added to `test_request_basic_fixture_maps_claim_with_diagnoses_and_no_response`. **A second, genuine bug in this reverse builder's own first draft was also caught before commit**: the dependent NM1 was built with `include_id=False`, copied unexamined from `edi_270.py`'s own precedent - but unlike 270's own real fixture (whose dependent commonly carries no member-specific id), 278's real dependent loop does carry one, and `_build_nm1_identifier` reads it whenever present regardless of role, so omitting it silently dropped real data on a round trip; fixed to `include_id=True`, see `tests/test_transform.py::test_edi_278_request_with_dependent_round_trip_preserves_both_patients`. Verified via all four real fixtures (both request fixtures' diagnoses/patient identifiers, both response fixtures' `outcome`/`preAuthRef`) and a 400-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 2 triggers) of `generate("EDI", trigger, seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures.
  - **`app/transform/remittance_835.py`** — `Edi835Builder`, registered as `("EDI", "835", "")`. The thirteenth slice, and the first EDI reverse slice for a family with **no `HL` hierarchy and no `BHT` segment at all** - a flat `BPR`/`TRN`/`N1`(payer)/`N1`(payee)/`CLP`... shape, genuinely simpler to reverse than every HL-hierarchy-based family this app has reversed so far, needing no loop-resolution helper at all. Reverses `app/edi/remittance_835.py::_build_organization_from_n1`/`_build_detail`/`Edi835Builder.build_bundle` field-for-field: `N1`'s own simpler shape (no first/last name split, built directly rather than reusing `edi_common.py`'s `NM1`-scoped helpers), `BPR02`/`BPR16` (payment amount/date), `TRN02` (trace number, `Bundle.identifier`'s own source here since there's no `BHT` to derive it from), and one `CLP` segment per `PaymentReconciliationDetail`. **Payer/payee resolution is more direct than any earlier EDI reverse slice**: `PaymentReconciliation.paymentIssuer`/`.requestor` are direct references to the payer/payee `Organization` resources - no Bundle-order fallback or exclusion logic needed at all, since both fields are always populated by the forward mapper and there's no third organization/HL loop to disambiguate against. **A real bug avoided by design, not caught after the fact**: this module does *not* reuse `app.transform.edi_common.reverse_nm1_qualifier` for N1's own id-qualifier reversal, since that function's fallback-marker prefix is NM1-scoped (`urn:interop-tools:x12-nm1-id:`) and genuinely differs from N1's own (`urn:interop-tools:x12-n1-id:`, promoted to public alongside `CLP_STATUS_SYSTEM`/`TRN_IDENTIFIER_SYSTEM` in `app/edi/remittance_835.py` for this reversal) - reusing it naively would have silently dropped a real fixture's own non-canonical `"XV"` qualifier, falling back to the wrong default instead of the correct one; a dedicated `_reverse_n1_qualifier` handles both the canonical-system and N1-specific-fallback-marker cases correctly. **Disclosed round-trip fidelity gaps**: `BPR01`/`BPR03`/`BPR04` and `CLP03`/`CLP05`/`CLP06`/`CLP07` have no FHIR-side home at all - the forward mapper never reads any of them - so each gets a fixed, disclosed placeholder on the way back out (`CLP03` mirrors `CLP04`'s own paid amount, the closest available real number, rather than a fabricated one); `CAS`/`SVC` are never regenerated either, matching the forward mapper's own disclosed "not modeled this phase" scope limit for both. Verified against both real fixtures (payment amount/date, claim identifiers/amounts, and the payer's own non-canonical `"XV"` qualifier all round-trip exactly) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("EDI", "835", seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures.
  - **`app/transform/claim_837p.py`** — `Edi837pBuilder`, registered as `("EDI", "837P", "")`. The fourteenth slice, and the first EDI reverse slice for the deepest, most cross-referential loop shape of any EDI phase this app has reversed so far: a claim-level `HI` diagnosis list cross-referenced by position from each service line's own diagnosis-pointer composite, plus a rendering provider (`Claim.careTeam[]`) distinct from the billing provider (`Claim.provider`). Reverses `app/edi/claim_837p.py::_build_service_line_item`/`_build_procedure_concept`/`_build_diagnosis_pointers`/`Edi837pBuilder.build_bundle` field-for-field: `CLM01`/`CLM02`/`CLM05` (claim id/total charge/place-of-service composite), `HI` (diagnosis composites, reusing `app.transform.edi_common.build_hi_segment`, its second real consumer, promoted there from `prior_auth.py` once this became the second real consumer of the identical single-HI-segment reversal), one `LX`+`SV1`(+`DTP`) group per `Claim.item[]`. **Payer/billing-provider/rendering-provider resolution is the simplest, most direct of any EDI reverse slice so far**: `Claim.provider`/`.insurer`/`.careTeam[].provider` are all real, direct references to their own resource - a plain by-id lookup across every `Organization`/`Practitioner` in the Bundle, keyed off each field's own real reference, with no fallback/exclusion logic needed at all. **Deliberately not a reuse of `edi_common.py`'s own `resolve_payer_and_provider`**: that function assumes exactly one payer + one non-payer participant to disambiguate, but 837P has a *third* non-payer participant (the rendering provider) that could just as easily be Practitioner-typed as the billing provider - reusing it would risk its own Organization-only-then-first-Practitioner fallback resolving to the wrong one. **Subscriber/dependent resolution reuses 270/271's own shared resolver directly**: 837P builds a real `Coverage` the identical way 278 does, and the identical structural simplification 278's own reverse slice already established applies again here (confirmed, not assumed, from `app.edi.common.Resolved837Loops`'s own docstring) - whenever a dependent exists at all, it's unconditionally also "the patient," so `dependent is not None` alone decides which loop the claim/service-line segments attach to. **Disclosed round-trip fidelity gaps**: `SV1-01`'s own procedure-code qualifier ambiguity (`"HC"` covers both CPT and HCPCS Level II) is preserved via the same disclosed local placeholder system (`PROCEDURE_QUALIFIER_FALLBACK_SYSTEM`, promoted to public in `app/edi/claim_837p.py` for this reversal) and reversed back to the real original qualifier when recoverable; `CLM03`/`CLM04`/`CLM06`-`CLM11`, `SV1-05`'s own per-line place-of-service override, and `2010AB`/`2310C`/`2320`/`2330` have no FHIR-side home at all and are omitted or fixed-placeholder, the same "no source field" precedent every earlier EDI reverse slice already established. Verified against both real fixtures (both service lines' procedure code/charge/diagnosis-pointers/care-team-sequence/serviced-date match exactly, both diagnoses match, and both patients survive the with-dependent fixture's own round trip) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("EDI", "837P", seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures.
  - **`app/transform/claim_837i.py`** — `Edi837iBuilder`, registered as `("EDI", "837I", "")`. The sixteenth slice, and the second real proof (after 837I's own forward direction) that 837P's own 3-level HL chain genuinely, not coincidentally, generalizes to a sibling variant. Reverses `app/edi/claim_837i.py::_build_service_line_item`/`_build_procedure_concept`/`_build_discharge_status_supporting_info`/`Edi837iBuilder.build_bundle` field-for-field: `CLM01`/`CLM02` (claim id/total charge - **not** `CLM05`, which 837I's own forward mapper never reads at all, a genuine structural fact, not an oversight carried over from 837P), `CL103` (discharge status, via `Claim.supportingInfo`), `HI` (diagnosis composites, reusing `build_hi_segment`, its third real consumer), one `LX`+`SV2`(+`DTP`) group per `Claim.item[]` - revenue code, optional procedure composite, charge, quantity, service date, **no** diagnosis-pointer composite, since `SV2` genuinely has none (confirmed directly by the forward module, not assumed from 837P's own `SV1-07`). **Resolution logic is identical to 837P's own, reused by the same disclosed reasoning**: `Claim.provider`/`.insurer`/`.careTeam[].provider` (here the *attending* provider, `NM1*71`, not 837P's rendering `NM1*82`) are all real, direct references, resolved via the identical by-id lookup `claim_837p.py`'s own reverse builder already established; subscriber/dependent resolution reuses 270/271's own shared resolver directly, via the identical `Coverage`-based disambiguation and "a dependent, when present, is unconditionally also the patient" simplification 837P's own reverse slice already established - confirmed, not assumed, since both variants share `app.edi.common.Resolved837Loops`'s own identical resolver. **Disclosed round-trip fidelity gaps, some inherited directly from the forward side, one genuinely new to this slice**: `CLM05-1`/`CL101`/`CL102`/`HI`'s own occurrence-value-condition-code usages have no FHIR-side home at all, the identical "no source field" precedent 837P's own reverse slice already established. **Genuinely new to this slice**: the forward mapper's own multi-`HI`-segment split (Principal Diagnosis in its own segment, Other Diagnosis composites in a second) is not reproduced - this builder always regenerates a single combined `HI` segment instead, since `iter_diagnosis_hi_segments` only checks a segment's *first* composite qualifier to decide inclusion, so one combined segment parses identically to several, unless a single claim ever carries more than 12 diagnoses (the per-segment composite cap), genuinely rare in practice and not exercised by either real 837I fixture. Verified against both real fixtures (both service lines' revenue code/procedure/charge/care-team-sequence/serviced-date, all three diagnoses, and the discharge status all match exactly on the basic fixture; the with-dependent fixture's own text-only "Revenue code X" fallback item and both patients survive too) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("EDI", "837I", seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures.
  - **`app/transform/claim_837d.py`** — `Edi837dBuilder`, registered as `("EDI", "837D", "")`. The seventeenth slice, and the one that completes the reverse direction for every EDI transaction-set family this app's forward direction supports - the full "big five" HIPAA suite (270/271, 276/277, 278, 835, 837P/837I/837D) now round-trips in both directions. Reverses `app/edi/claim_837d.py::_build_service_line_item`/`_build_procedure_concept`/`_build_tooth_body_site`/`_build_tooth_sub_sites`/`resolve_line_dtp_raw_date`/`Edi837dBuilder.build_bundle` field-for-field: `CLM01`/`CLM02`/`CLM05` (claim id/total charge/place-of-service composite - the SAME vocabulary as 837P's, confirmed by the forward module's own docstring, unlike 837I's genuinely different UB-04 one), `HI` (diagnosis composites, reusing `build_hi_segment`, its fourth real consumer), one `LX`+`SV3`(+`TOO`)(+`DTP`) group per `Claim.item[]` (CDT procedure composite, charge, quantity, diagnosis-pointer composite, tooth number/surfaces, service date). **This slice is also what finally promoted the reverse direction's own by-id resolution helpers**: `_resolve_by_reference`/`_org_or_person_nm1` had been independently duplicated, byte-for-byte identical, in both `claim_837p.py` and `claim_837i.py` (each disclosed at the time as "not yet promoted, pending a third real consumer" - see `app/edi/common.py::Resolved837Loops`'s own docstring for the identical forward-side precedent, which itself wasn't promoted until 837D became ITS third consumer either) - 837D is that third consumer, so both helpers are now `app.transform.edi_common.resolve_by_reference`/`org_or_person_nm1`, with `claim_837p.py`/`claim_837i.py` both updated to call the shared versions instead of keeping their own copies. **Two genuinely new pieces of reversal logic neither 837P nor 837I needed**: the procedure-code qualifier defaults to `"AD"` (CDT), not 837P's/837I's own `"HC"` default, reusing the identical disclosed-fallback-marker pattern via `app.edi.claim_837p.PROCEDURE_QUALIFIER_FALLBACK_SYSTEM` directly (confirmed identical between the two forward modules, not assumed) alongside the newly-public `app.edi.claim_837d.CDT_CODE_SYSTEM`;
