- **`app/cda/history_and_physical.py`** — `HistoryAndPhysicalBuilder` (`HISTORY_AND_PHYSICAL_TEMPLATE_ID = "2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.1.3"`): the third document type, and a second real proof (after Discharge Summary) that `build_sectioned_bundle` generalizes - `build_bundle()` is, like the other two builders', just `return build_sectioned_bundle(document)`. Confirmed structurally identical to CCD/Discharge Summary at the header level (`recordTarget`, `componentOf/encompassingEncounter`) by fetching a real HL7 C-CDA-Examples History and Physical document and comparing directly - the same fetch that surfaced the Procedures "entries optional" gap fixed above. **A real, disclosed scope limit, not a redesign - mirroring Discharge Summary's own precedent exactly**: an H&P Note's own IG-required narrative sections (Reason for Visit/Chief Complaint, History of Present Illness, Physical Exam, Assessment, Plan of Care, Review of Systems, Social History, Family History, General Status) either carry no structured entries at all (narrative-only, would need a proper FHIR Composition, already out-of-scope) or use entry shapes this app's existing section builders don't recognize - none are mapped this slice, silently skipped the same way. Verified directly against the real fetched example (not assumed): alongside these H&P-specific sections, it also carried Allergies, Immunizations, Medications, Problems, Procedures, Results, and Vital Signs sections - **every one of this app's seven currently-recognized section types in a single document** - so a real H&P Note converts to something genuinely useful (header + all seven) even without the H&P-specific narrative ones. Unlike Discharge Summary (`force_encounter=True` in the generator, since a discharge is inherently tied to one hospitalization), an H&P's own `componentOf/encompassingEncounter` is genuinely optional in the generator too - a real H&P can precede any admission (e.g. a pre-operative visit), matching CCD's own optional-encounter treatment rather than Discharge Summary's forced one.
- **Format auto-detection (`app/pipeline.py`)** — `is_xml(raw_text)` strips a leading BOM **before** whitespace (not after: the BOM character isn't itself whitespace, so a leading BOM stops a whitespace-only `lstrip()` before it ever reaches the whitespace that follows it — this exact ordering bug was caught by direct testing before shipping), then checks for a leading `<`. `convert_to_bundle`/`validate_any` dispatch to `convert_cda_to_bundle`/`convert_hl7_to_bundle` and `validate_cda`/`validate_hl7` accordingly. This keeps the single unified "paste anything, click Convert/Validate" UX every prior HL7v2 message type has used — never a new route/UI branch per input format.
- **`app/cda/generator.py`** — `generate_ccd(rng)`/`generate_discharge_summary(rng) -> str`, the C-CDA mirror of `app/generators/{adt,siu,oru,mdm}.py`, but living inside `app/cda/` rather than `app/generators/`: there are only two document types so far, so a separate `app/generators/cda.py` with its own sub-registry would be premature relative to HL7v2's real 4-type/20-trigger registry need. Both generator functions are thin wrappers around a shared `_generate_sectioned_document(rng, document_template_id, doc_code, doc_code_display, title, force_encounter=False)` - the generator-side mirror of `build_sectioned_bundle` above, extracted the same "second real consumer" way once `generate_discharge_summary` existed. `force_encounter=True` for Discharge Summary (unlike CCD, where `componentOf/encompassingEncounter` is genuinely optional and stays a 50/50 `maybe()`) - a real discharge summary is inherently tied to one hospitalization, so `_random_encounter` gained a `force: bool` parameter to skip its own presence check when set. Reuses `app/generators/base.py`'s format-agnostic primitives directly (`maybe()`, `random_person_name()`, `random_address()`, `random_phone()`, `random_sex()`, `random_identifier()`, `random_datetime_near_now()`/`format_hl7_datetime()` - the latter's `YYYYMMDDHHMMSS` output is exactly CDA's own `@value` TS digit shape) rather than re-deriving name/address pools; net-new is only a small SNOMED problem-code pool, an RxNorm medication-code pool (plus a small route-code and free-text-SIG pool for the Medications section), and the XML field builders. Built via f-string templates (same style as the hand-authored `ccd_*.xml` fixtures), not `ElementTree` tree-building, and self-checked via `parse_document()` before returning (mirrors `_assemble()`'s `parse_message()` self-check in the HL7v2 generators) - full-conversion-success verification is left to `tests/test_generate_cda.py`, matching the HL7v2 precedent. **Every random value is drawn from the passed-in `rng`, never `uuid.uuid4()`** - `uuid4()` uses its own unseeded internal randomness and would silently break the "same seed → same output byte-for-byte" guarantee every generator in this project must uphold; `_random_uuid_like(rng)` builds a UUID-shaped root string entirely from `rng.choice()` calls instead. Field-population policy deliberately exercises both branches of everything the review-caught `common.py`/`problems.py` bugs above involved (root-only vs. root+extension `id`s, date-only vs. full-timestamp `effectiveTime`, recognized vs. unrecognized encounter class, Concern-Act-only vs. nested-Status-Observation `clinicalStatus`), while keeping onset/birth date ranges constrained so no combination can ever trip an error-severity validation rule (see the file's own comments for the exact day-count bounds and why) - the same "guarantee convertibility/validity by construction" contract the HL7v2 generators already keep. `_random_medication_entry` follows the identical philosophy for the Medications section: `moodCode` splits ~50/50 INT/EVN, `statusCode` is mostly a recognized `STATUS_MAP` key (~85%) with an occasional unrecognized one (`new`/`held`, exercising the `"unknown"`-fallback validation finding), and dosing representation splits ~45/35/20 across structured-only/free-text-only/neither - direct fuzz coverage of `_build_dosage`'s three branches, mirroring how the Problems entry generator exercises `_resolve_clinical_status`'s two branches. `_random_allergy_entry` covers the Allergies section's larger branch space the same way: ~10% `negationInd`, and within that ~30% still carrying a resolvable allergen vs. a `nullFlavor` one (both `_resolve_allergen_code` negation paths); the Status Observation (~50%), Criticality Observation (~40%), and Reaction Observation with its own optional nested Severity Observation (~50%/~60%) are each independently `maybe()`-gated, so every nesting depth this section supports gets exercised in some combination across seeds. `_random_immunization_entry` splits `moodCode` ~80/20 EVN/INT (direct fuzz coverage of `build_immunizations()`'s mood-based skip - the INT branch must produce zero `Immunization` resources from that entry), and within EVN further splits ~15% negated; `statusCode` is mostly a recognized `STATUS_MAP` key with an occasional unrecognized one (`"draft"`, exercising the `"completed"`-fallback validation finding); dosing (`routeCode`+`doseQuantity`) and `lotNumberText` are each independently `maybe()`-gated. Registered in the *existing* `app/generators/registry.py::_GENERATORS` flat dict as `("CDA", "CCD")`/`("CDA", "DISCHARGESUMMARY")` (see above) rather than a parallel CDA-specific registry - `DISCHARGESUMMARY` is uppercase (unlike the human-readable `"DischargeSummary"` label) to match `generate()`'s own `.upper()`-normalized lookup, which requires its dict keys to already be uppercase, same as every HL7v2 trigger value.
- **`app/cda/validation.py`** — `validate_document(document) -> ValidationReport`, the C-CDA mirror of `app/validation/{generic,adt,engine}.py`, composed the same small-`_rule_*`-functions-by-concatenation way. Still no `get_type_validator`-style dispatch-by-document-type registry even with two document types now - every section-level rule (Problems/Medications/Allergies/Immunizations) walks the document generically (`component/structuredBody/component/section`, exactly what `build_sectioned_bundle` walks too) rather than branching on which document type it's in, so a CCD and a Discharge Summary carrying the same recognized section get the same findings for free; only `_resolve_trigger_event(document)` needs to know about both document types by name (checks `CCD_TEMPLATE_ID` then `DISCHARGE_SUMMARY_TEMPLATE_ID`, returning `"CCD"`/`"DISCHARGESUMMARY"`/`None` - uppercase to match every other trigger-event string in this app and `app/generators/registry.py::generate()`'s own normalized lookup). Reuses `app.validation.models.ValidationFinding`/`ValidationReport` as-is (a CDA finding uses a path-like string for `segment`, e.g. `"recordTarget/patientRole/patient/administrativeGenderCode"`, `field` left `None`) and `app.validation.common.parse_comparable_datetime`/`not_in_future`/`is_before` directly, since CDA's `@value` uses the identical HL7-TS digit shape. Rules mirror `adt.py`'s explicit "surface what the mapper already silently defaults/skips" philosophy: a Problem Observation with no resolvable coded value is an `info` finding ("the converter will silently skip this entry") even though `build_conditions()` itself never raises for it; an unrecognized encounter class code is `info` ("will silently default to AMB"), reusing `app.cda.common.RECOGNIZED_ENCOUNTER_CLASSES` directly rather than a re-derived copy. The Medications section follows the same pattern via `_iter_medication_activities` (walking entries the identical way `build_medication_requests()` does, so validation can never see a different entry set than conversion) and three rules: `cda.medication-missing-code` (`info` - mirrors the Problem Observation missing-value rule exactly, skipped for negated entries since the converter itself never evaluates those), `cda.medication-status-unrecognized` (`info` - reuses `app.cda.medications.STATUS_MAP` directly, same "will silently default to X" wording as the encounter-class rule), and `cda.medication-period-end-before-start` (`error` - dosing `effectiveTime` high before low, reusing `is_before` the same way the Problems abatement-before-onset and Encounter period-end-before-start rules already do). Allergies gets the same treatment via `_iter_allergy_observations` and four rules: `cda.allergy-missing-allergen` (`info` - skipped for negated entries, since `_resolve_allergen_code` gives those a real text-only fallback rather than skipping them, unlike every other "missing code" rule in this file), `cda.allergy-onset-in-future` (`warning`) and `cda.allergy-onset-before-birth` (`error`) mirroring Problems' onset rules exactly, and `cda.allergy-reaction-missing-manifestation` (`info` - a Reaction Observation with no resolvable `<value>`, surfacing `_build_reaction`'s own skip-the-reaction behavior). Immunizations gets four rules via `_iter_evn_immunization_activities` (which, like the mapper's own walk, excludes `INT`-mood entries entirely rather than flagging them - a scope limit, not a data-quality issue): `cda.immunization-missing-vaccine-code` (`info`), `cda.immunization-status-unrecognized` (`info` - only evaluated for non-negated entries, since `negationInd="true"` overrides `status` unconditionally in the mapper regardless of `statusCode`'s value), `cda.immunization-occurrence-in-future` (`warning`), and `cda.immunization-occurrence-before-birth` (`error` - mirroring Problems'/Allergies' own onset-before-birth rules exactly; a follow-up review found `_rule_immunizations` was the one section-level rule of the three not threading `patient` through to its own call site at all, an omission rather than a structural limitation, since `patient` was already resolved and in scope in `validate_document` - see `tests/test_cda_validation.py::test_immunization_occurrence_before_birth_is_error`). Convertibility is checked last, mirroring `app/validation/engine.py::_check_convertibility()` exactly: call `get_document_builder(document)` (unregistered → `info` finding), then `.build_bundle(document)` (`MappingError`/`MissingSegmentError`/`pydantic.ValidationError` → `error` finding; anything else → logged + `error` finding) - the same load-bearing "never re-derive a second required-fields table, just run the real builder" decision already made for HL7v2. `app/cda/pipeline.py::validate_cda(raw_xml)` parses then delegates, mirroring `convert_cda_to_bundle`'s exact shape - it raises only `CdaParseError` (unlike `validate_hl7`, there's no second raise-worthy case: HL7v2's "MSH itself absent" maps to a malformed/wrong-root document here, already `CdaParseError` from `parse_document`). **`_rule_encounter`'s period-timing checks originally lived entirely inside `if low:`**, so an `IVL_TS` with only `@high` (known end, unknown start - a legal shape `ivl_ts_bounds()` returns as `(None, high)`) silently produced *zero* encounter findings even when the known end date was implausibly in the future - caught by a follow-up multi-agent code review, reproduced directly. Fixed by computing `low_dt`/`high_dt` independently up front and adding a dedicated `cda.encounter-period-end-in-future` check for the no-`low`/future-`high` case, rather than nesting everything under `low`'s truthiness.
- **Known, disclosed scope limits for this slice**: three document types (CCD, Discharge Summary, History and Physical Note), nine sections recognized total - seven general-purpose ones (Problems, Medications, Allergies, Immunizations, Vital Signs, Results, Procedures) recognized on any of the three document types, plus two Discharge-Summary-specific ones (Hospital Discharge Diagnosis, Discharge Medications, see `hospital_discharge_diagnosis.py`/`discharge_medications.py`) - Discharge Summary's remaining two required sections (Hospital Course, Plan of Treatment) not yet recognized (see `discharge_summary.py`), History and Physical's own required narrative sections (Reason for Visit, History of Present Illness, Physical Exam, Assessment, Plan of Care, Review of Systems, Social History, Family History, General Status) not yet recognized (see `history_and_physical.py`), Vital Signs' Blood Pressure/Pulse Oximetry component-grouping not yet implemented (see `vitals.py`), Results' `category`/`specimen`/`author`->Provenance and `IVL_PQ`/`ED`-typed values not yet mapped (see `results.py`), Procedures' `performer`/`participant`/`author`->Provenance and Indication/Comment Activity cross-references not yet mapped (see `procedures.py`), no proper FHIR-Document `Bundle(type="document")`/`Composition` (author/custodian would become load-bearing), no narrative-`<text>`/`originalText` resolution when `displayName` is absent (falls back to code-only). All are natural, already-scoped follow-ups on the infrastructure above, not redesigns.

### EDI/X12 support (`app/edi/`)

X12 EDI is the app's third input *format* and, unlike C-CDA (self-describing XML), a genuinely different parsing problem again: a delimited text format whose own delimiters aren't fixed by spec but are declared *by the file itself*, at fixed byte positions inside its own envelope header (`ISA`). Sibling package to `app/hl7/`/`app/cda/`, not folded into either. Phase 1 covers **270** (Eligibility Inquiry) and **271** (Eligibility Response) - chosen because they're structurally the simplest of the "big five" healthcare X12 transaction sets (no claim/service-line hierarchy) and map to FHIR's own base-spec `CoverageEligibilityRequest`/`CoverageEligibilityResponse` with no extension IG required.

**A disclosed gap versus the other two formats**: HL7v2 has the ballot-published `v2-to-FHIR` IG and C-CDA has `C-CDA on FHIR`, both with per-field ConceptMaps this project leans on for verification. **No equivalent official, free X12-to-FHIR ConceptMap IG exists** - X12's own authoritative TR3 Implementation Guides are paywalled. Every field mapping in `eligibility_270.py`/`eligibility_271.py` is instead checked against the base (free) FHIR resource definitions plus a hand-verified real 270/271 segment/loop shape (HL03 level codes, NM1/DMG/EQ/EB/DTP/AAA positions) - each module's own docstring says so explicitly, the same disclosure discipline as every MDM/TXA and CDA field-mapping decision above.

- **`app/edi/parser.py`** — the `app/hl7/parser.py`/`app/cda/parser.py` equivalent for X12. Two real gotchas its primitives are built to make structurally impossible to hit by accident: (1) **`ISA` is the only fixed-width segment** (106 bytes, spec-guaranteed) - `read_isa_delimiters(raw_text) -> Delimiters` reads the element separator (byte 3, immediately after the literal `"ISA"`), repetition separator (byte 82, ISA11 - captured but not interpreted this slice, no Phase-1 field needs it), component separator (byte 104, ISA16), and segment terminator (byte 105) purely by position, never by splitting on a guessed delimiter - and this byte-position trick must never be generalized to any other segment, including `ISA`'s own trailer `IEA`. (2) **Trailing omitted elements are normal, not malformed** (e.g. a segment ending `...**100*95~` with an empty element) - `element(segment, index)` (1-based, matching X12's own numbering where element 1 is `segment[1]`) and `component(value, delimiters, index)` (1-based sub-element access into a composite value) are both bounds-guarded to return `""` rather than raise, the same contract `app/hl7/parser.py::field_str` already provides for HL7 fields. `split_segments()` strips surrounding whitespace from each segment before splitting into elements - real-world files commonly emit a line break after the segment terminator despite the spec not calling for one, and without this the stray newline glues onto the next segment's first element and silently breaks every subsequent segment-ID match. `parse_interchange(raw_text) -> Interchange` walks segments by **matching on segment ID** (`ISA`/`GS`/`ST`/`SE`/`GE`/`IEA`) to reconstruct the envelope tree, deliberately **not** by trusting the trailer segments' own declared counts (`IEA02`/`GE01`/`SE01`) - a wrong count is a plausible real-world sender bug that shouldn't block parsing, so `EdiParseError` is raised only when the envelope's nesting is genuinely broken (a boundary segment with no matching opener, an opener never closed before its parent closes, a missing `IEA`) - count *mismatches* become `warning`-severity validation findings instead (see `validation.py` below), the X12 analogue of "never raise for something a real sender could plausibly produce." An interchange that's well-formed but contains zero transaction sets is not an error either - `first_transaction_set(interchange)` returns `None`, and `app/edi/pipeline.py::convert_edi_to_bundle` is what turns that into a `MissingSegmentError` (there's genuinely nothing to convert), mirroring how CDA's "root isn't `ClinicalDocument`" and HL7v2's "`MSH` itself absent" are each raised by the layer that actually needed the missing thing, not by the parser speculatively. Two loop-grouping primitives: `group_by_leader(segments, leader_id, member_ids)` is a direct `list[Segment]`-typed port of `app/hl7/parser.py::group_segments_by_leader`'s exact algorithm (used for 270's `EQ`/271's `EB` leading their `REF`/`DTP`/`MSG` members); `group_by_hl_hierarchy(segments) -> list[HlLoop]` is genuinely new - nothing in HL7v2 or C-CDA needs this shape, since X12's `HL` segments encode a tree via **sibling pointers** (`HL01` this loop's own id, `HL02` parent's id, `HL03` level code - e.g. `"20"`=Information Source/`"21"`=Information Receiver/`"22"`=Subscriber/`"23"`=Dependent for 270/271, `HL04` has-children flag) rather than XML's native nesting or a simple leader/member run. Two passes: build a flat `HlLoop` per `HL` segment (every following non-`HL` segment up to the next `HL` is that loop's `member_segments`), then link `.children` by matching `hl02` → some other loop's `hl01`; a loop whose `hl02` doesn't resolve to any `hl01` (including a genuinely root loop, whose `hl02` is simply absent) becomes a root rather than being dropped or raising - a safe, disclosed default for a malformed sibling pointer, surfaced separately as `edi.hl-parent-not-found` in validation instead. `find_child_loop(loop, hl03)` (in `common.py`, not `parser.py`, since it's 270/271-shape-specific) walks `.children` the same way `app/cda/parser.py::find_child` does for CDA. `parse_decimal(raw) -> Decimal | None` (added Phase 4, for 835's money fields) parses defensively via `try/except InvalidOperation` like every other numeric primitive here, plus one extra guard neither `_int_or_none` nor a naive `Decimal(raw)` call has: **`Decimal("NaN")`/`Decimal("Infinity")`/`Decimal("-Infinity")` do not raise `InvalidOperation`** - Python's own `decimal` module parses those literal strings successfully, so a malformed-but-decimal-shaped X12 money element (`CLP03`/`CLP04`/`BPR02`) could otherwise flow a non-finite value straight into a `>`/`==` comparison or a constructed `Money.value` uncaught. `parse_decimal` explicitly rejects a non-finite result via `.is_finite()` after parsing, returning `None` the same as any other unparseable input. `remittance_835.py::_parse_money` and every 835 validation rule in `validation.py` share this one implementation rather than each having its own `try/except Decimal` - a follow-up code review caught that the first version of `validation.py`'s three 835 rules had their own local `_decimal_or_none()` with no non-finite guard - `Decimal('NaN') > Decimal('1')` itself raises `InvalidOperation` (confirmed directly, not assumed), so `_rule_835_claim_paid_exceeds_charge`'s `paid > charge` comparison would have crashed uncaught for a `CLP03`/`CLP04` value spelled `"NaN"`, turning `/api/validate` into exactly the kind of raw 500 this project has explicitly disclosed and fixed before (see the "date-only PID-7" and "calendar-invalid date" entries in the validation extension-point section above) - fixed by switching all three rules to the shared `parse_decimal`, see `tests/test_remittance_validation.py::test_835_non_finite_clp_amounts_do_not_crash_validation`.
- **`app/edi/common.py`** — the `app/cda/common.py` equivalent: cross-transaction-set shared builders, since NM1/DMG shape and Bundle/date assembly are standardized across X12 healthcare transaction sets generally, not 270/271-specific (a future 276/277 builder reuses this directly). `parse_x12_datetime(date_str, time_str="") -> str | None` concatenates X12's separately-carried date (`CCYYMMDD`) and time (`HHMM[SS]`) elements and delegates straight to the existing `app.fhir_models.builders.parse_hl7_datetime`/`parse_hl7_date` - X12's digit shapes are byte-for-byte the same as HL7v2's TS fields, the same direct-reuse opportunity `app/cda/common.py::parse_partial_ts` already took for CDA; falls back to a date-only result when `time_str` is empty or the combined string doesn't parse as a full datetime, returning `None` only when `date_str` itself is empty/invalid. `build_organization_from_nm1`/`build_practitioner_from_nm1`/`build_patient_from_nm1_dmg` build the payer/provider/subscriber-or-dependent resources from an `NM1` (+ optional `DMG`) segment; `is_person_entity(nm1)` (NM102 `"1"`=Person vs `"2"`=Non-Person Entity) is what both eligibility builders use to decide whether the 2000B provider loop materializes a `Practitioner` or an `Organization`, since X12 allows either there. `assemble_bundle(bht, *resources) -> Bundle` is the X12-header equivalent of `app/cda/common.py::assemble_bundle` (which reads `ClinicalDocument` fields instead): `BHT03` → `Bundle.identifier`, `BHT04`+`BHT05` → `Bundle.timestamp`, `Bundle.type = "collection"` matching every existing pipeline's output shape. BHT (the transaction's own application-level reference) is used rather than the ISA envelope's `ISA13` interchange control number, since BHT03 is more meaningful to a consumer than an interchange-level control number. **The exact `Bundle.timestamp`-is-`instant`-has-no-date-only-form bug already disclosed for CDA above was proactively avoided here, not independently rediscovered**: `assemble_bundle` calls `parse_hl7_datetime` directly (no date-only fallback) for `Bundle.timestamp` rather than `parse_x12_datetime`, whose whole purpose is that fallback - using the latter would crash resource construction on an otherwise perfectly convertible transaction set whenever `BHT05` (time) is empty or unparseable, the identical failure mode CDA's `assemble_bundle` shipped once and fixed. Two disclosed local-system fallbacks, same category as CDA's `_OID_TO_FHIR_SYSTEM` and MDM's TXA-3 table: `SERVICE_TYPE_CODE_SYSTEM` (no FHIR-canonical URI exists for X12's own Service Type Code external code list, used by both 270's `EQ01` and 271's `EB03`) and `NM1_ID_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` (public - NM108 Identification Code Qualifier → a canonical system where one is officially recognized - `XX`→NPI, `SY`→SSN - falling back to a disclosed local system keyed by the qualifier itself for anything else, e.g. `PI`/`MI`; NM108 is normalized `.strip().upper()` before this lookup, matching DMG03's own normalization three lines below in the same file - a lowercase qualifier shipped once without this and silently fell through to the disclosed per-qualifier fallback instead of the canonical NPI/SSN system, caught by a follow-up code review). The lookup-with-fallback logic itself is `resolve_id_qualifier_system(qualifier, fallback_system) -> str`, extracted once `remittance_835.py::_build_n1_identifier` became a second real consumer of the identical qualifier→system resolution `_build_nm1_identifier` already did inline (N103/N104 use the same Identification Code Qualifier list as NM108, just at a different segment position - see that module's own bullet) - both callers now share one implementation instead of two independently-drifting copies. `_DMG_GENDER_MAP` (X12 code list 1068, DMG03: `M`/`F`/`U`) is its own table, not a reuse of HL7v2 PID-8's `M`/`F`/`O` map - a genuinely different vocabulary, the same "different-but-similar-looking vocabulary needs its own table" situation CDA's `F`/`M`/`UN` gender vocabulary already was relative to HL7v2's. `BHT_REFERENCE_SYSTEM` is the one named constant behind every `Reference`-by-identifier back to BHT03 in this app (both `assemble_bundle`'s own `Bundle.identifier` and `eligibility_271.py`'s `.request` fallback - see that module's bullet below) - promoted here after the two uses were found independently re-typing the same literal string, risking silent drift if one were ever changed without the other. **`resolve_eligibility_parties(segments, transaction_set_id) -> ResolvedEligibilityParties`** is the single real implementation of "walk 2000A→2000B→2000C→2000D and decide which NM1/DMG pair is the patient" - both `eligibility_270.py` and `eligibility_271.py`'s `build_bundle()` call it instead of each independently re-deriving the ~40-line walk, and `app/edi/eligibility_validation.py`'s own 270/271 plausibility rules call it too (wrapped in a `try/except MissingSegmentError` there, since validation must never raise). This was extracted after a follow-up multi-agent code review caught that the two builders' copies had already drifted apart from a third, independent copy inside `validation.py` - the validator's own version used a loose whole-tree search for the subscriber loop (instead of the strict 2000A→2000B→2000C parent chain `find_child_loop` enforces) and treated any present 2000D loop as "the patient" without checking its NM1 actually resolved, so a malformed-but-plausible dependent loop (HL03="23" present, no NM1) could make validation inspect a completely different, and empty, segment set than what conversion actually used - see `tests/test_eligibility_271_mapping.py::test_dependent_loop_without_nm1_falls_back_to_subscriber` and `tests/test_edi_validation.py::test_271_no_service_type_rule_follows_dependent_only_when_nm1_resolves` for the paired regression tests proving both sides now agree. **`build_coverage(patient, payer, subscriber) -> Coverage`** is a minimal, always-`"active"` `Coverage` linking beneficiary/payor/subscriber - independently hand-written the identical way three separate times (`eligibility_270.py`, `eligibility_271.py`, `prior_auth.py`) before being promoted here once `claim_837p.py` became a fourth real consumer of the exact same six-line shape; all four builders now call this instead. **`build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts(hi, delimiters) -> list[CodeableConcept]`** and its `HI_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` table parse `HI`'s repeating diagnosis-code composites (`HI*ABK:J209*ABF:E119~` = two diagnoses, not two segments) - promoted from `prior_auth.py`'s own Phase 3 `_build_diagnoses`/`_HI_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` once `claim_837p.py` needed the identical parsing loop a second time. **This promotion caught a real, pre-existing factual error, not just a duplication**: Phase 3's original table mapped qualifier `"BF"` itself to `http://hl7.org/fhir/sid/icd-10-cm` - verified directly (not assumed) against X12 code list 1270 that this is wrong: `"BF"`/`"BK"` are the legacy **ICD-9-CM** other/principal-diagnosis qualifiers, while `"ABF"`/`"ABK"` are the 5010 **ICD-10-CM** ones. The corrected `HI_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` maps `"ABK"`/`"ABF"` to `http://hl7.org/fhir/sid/icd-10-cm` and `"BK"`/`"BF"` to the real (also verified) `http://hl7.org/fhir/sid/icd-9-cm`, rather than guessing either was interchangeable with the other - see `tests/test_edi_common.py` (direct unit tests for the corrected table) and `tests/test_prior_auth_mapping.py`'s updated assertion (the existing `edi_278_request_basic.x12` fixture happens to use the legacy `"BF"` qualifier, so its diagnosis now correctly resolves to ICD-9-CM, not ICD-10-CM as the pre-fix test wrongly asserted).
- **`app/edi/base.py`** / **`app/edi/registry.py`** — `EdiTransactionBuilder(ABC)` (`build_bundle(transaction_set, delimiters) -> Bundle`) is the `MessageMapper`/`CdaDocumentBuilder` equivalent: transaction sets (270/271/276/277/278/835/837) are a "different shape dispatched via registry" situation, same as HL7v2 message types and C-CDA document types. **`delimiters` was added to the interface in Phase 2**, alongside `transaction_set` rather than folded into it, since it's an interchange-level concern shared by every transaction set in the interchange - `claim_status.py` was the first builder needing it, to split STC01's composite category/status sub-parts via `app.edi.parser::component()`, which requires the interchange's own self-declared component separator (270/271 never needed it, since every field either eligibility builder reads is a simple element, not a composite). `app/edi/pipeline.py::convert_edi_to_bundle` passes `interchange.delimiters`; so does `app/edi/validation.py::_check_convertibility`, which needed the same one-parameter addition. `Edi270Builder`/`Edi271Builder`/`Edi835Builder` all accept the parameter but don't use it (835 has no composite element to parse either), purely to satisfy the shared interface; `Edi837pBuilder`/`Edi837iBuilder` are the third and fourth builders (after `claim_status.py`/`prior_auth.py`) that genuinely need it, to split `CLM05`/`SV1-01`/`SV1-07` (837P) and `CLM05`/`SV2-02` (837I)'s own composite sub-parts. `registry.py`'s `_TRANSACTION_BUILDERS` is keyed by `ST01` (`"270"`/`"271"`/`"276"`/`"277"`/`"278"`/`"835"`) - X12's own transaction-set-code axis is already the CDA-templateId-equivalent single dimension, no second axis needed the way HL7v2's `(message_type, trigger_event)` pair is, **with two real exceptions, each resolved a different way**: `"278"` is a single registry *entry* for both 278 request and response, since they share one `ST01` and the request/response branch happens inside `Edi278Builder.build_bundle()` itself by reading `BHT02` (see `prior_auth.py`'s own module docstring); `"837"` isn't in the dict *at all* - unlike 278, there's no field inside the transaction set itself to branch on, so `get_transaction_builder(st01: str, st03: str = "")` intercepts `"837"` before the dict is ever consulted and dispatches on `st03` instead (see `claim_837i.py`'s own bullet above for the full research and reasoning behind choosing `ST03` over `GS08` for this). `get_transaction_builder` raises `MappingError` if `st01` isn't registered at all, mirroring `get_document_builder`/`get_mapper` exactly (including the `.strip().upper()` key normalization, currently inert since every registered `ST01` is purely numeric, but kept consistent with the sibling registries rather than silently diverging).
- **Why 270 and 271 are two sibling builder files, not a `Base*` + subclass pair**: unlike ADT/SIU trigger events or CCD/DischargeSummary (which share nearly everything down to one literal `build_sectioned_bundle` call), 270 and 271 are a genuine request/response pair with a different leader segment (`EQ` vs `EB`) and an entirely different target FHIR resource - the "genuinely different structure, don't force it through one shared helper" case CDA's own `build_sectioned_bundle` docstring already warns about. What's actually shared - envelope parsing, the full 2000A→2000B→2000C→2000D HL-hierarchy walk and patient-precedence resolution (`common.py::resolve_eligibility_parties`), NM1/DMG builders, `Bundle` assembly - lives in `parser.py`/`common.py`; both builder modules call it directly rather than one subclassing the other. Only the patient-loop leader (`EQ`/`EB`) and the target resource construction that follows are genuinely per-transaction-set and stay in each builder's own file.
- **`app/edi/eligibility_270.py`** — `Edi270Builder` → `CoverageEligibilityRequest`. Loop shape (all HL03-keyed): 2000A (`"20"`, `NM1*PR`) → payer `Organization`; 2000B (`"21"`, `NM1*1P`/`41`) → provider `Organization`/`Practitioner` (branches on `is_person_entity`); 2000C (`"22"`, `NM1*IL`+`DMG`) → subscriber `Patient`; 2000D (`"23"`, `NM1*QC`+`DMG`, optional) → dependent `Patient`. **Named precedence rule, not left implicit**: `.patient` = the 2000D dependent when present *and its own NM1 resolves*, else the 2000C subscriber themselves - enforced once, by `common.py::resolve_eligibility_parties`, not re-derived per builder (documented in the module docstring the same way ADT/SIU's own field-precedence rules are). The nested 2110C/2110D loop leader `EQ` (`EQ01` Service Type Code) → `CoverageEligibilityRequest.item[].category` (via the shared `build_service_type_category`); its `DTP` (qualifier `"D8"` only - a plain `CCYYMMDD` date; the range qualifier `"RD8"` is deferred, disclosed rather than guessed at) → `servicedDate`, a single top-level field (confirmed by inspecting `model_fields` directly, not assumed) that takes the first resolvable date found across every `EQ` group - a disclosed simplification for the common case where one 270 carries one uniform service date. `.purpose` (FHIR-required - see the `fhir.resources` gotcha list above) has no source field at all in 270/271 ("why are we asking" isn't a data element X12 carries here) - defaults to `DEFAULT_PURPOSE = "benefits"` (the dominant real-world use), the same "default to the most common real value when no unknown option exists" judgment already made for Medications' `moodCode`/Immunizations' `statusCode` fallbacks. `.created` (also FHIR-required) is a real business-rule requirement, not just a default: resolved from `BHT04`(+`05`) via `parse_x12_datetime`, and `Edi270Builder` raises `MappingError` if it doesn't resolve - the same "require the one field with no safe default" precedent `AdtA03Mapper` already set for discharge time.
- **`app/edi/eligibility_271.py`** — `Edi271Builder` → `CoverageEligibilityResponse`. Same envelope/HL/NM1/DMG walk and dependent-precedence rule as 270, via the same shared `common.py::resolve_eligibility_parties` (`BHT02="11"` distinguishes a 271 from 270's `BHT02="13"`); diverges where 271's own segments differ: the 2110C/2110D leader is `EB` (Eligibility/Benefit Information), not `EQ` - `EB03` → `item.category` (same shared `build_service_type_category`), `EB01` (normalized `.strip().upper()` before lookup, matching `EB12`'s own normalization a few lines below - a lowercase EB01 shipped once without this and silently left `.excluded` unset/`.inforce` stuck `False`, caught by a follow-up code review, see `tests/test_eligibility_271_mapping.py::test_lowercase_eb01_still_resolves_excluded`) → `item.excluded` via a disclosed subset (`_EB01_EXCLUDED_MAP`: `"1"`→Active/`False`, `"6"`→Inactive/`True`, `"I"`→Non-Covered/`True`; unrecognized codes leave `.excluded` unset rather than guessed at) and also drives `insurance.inforce` (`True` if any `EB01="1"` was seen across the patient's `EB` groups), `EB05` → `item.description` (free text), `EB12` (Y/N/U) → `item.network` as disclosed free text via `_EB12_NETWORK_TEXT` (no official FHIR CodeSystem exists for this indicator - `network` is itself a `CodeableConcept`, confirmed by inspecting `model_fields`, so text-only is a legitimate, disclosed choice, the same treatment Allergies' "no known allergy" text fallback already established). `AAA` (Request Validation) segments are scanned across the **whole transaction set**, not loop-scoped (a disclosed Phase-1 simplification) - any `AAA01="N"` → `.outcome = "error"`, **unconditionally on whether `AAA03` itself resolved** (an earlier version required a non-empty `AAA03` before treating the rejection as real, silently reporting `"complete"` for a genuinely rejected request whose reason code just didn't resolve - caught by a follow-up code review, see `tests/test_eligibility_271_mapping.py::test_aaa_rejection_with_empty_reason_code_still_sets_error_outcome`) - and `.disposition` = free text built from whichever `AAA03` reject-reason codes did resolve (no verified FHIR CodeSystem crosswalk exists for `AAA03`, so it's carried as text rather than guessed at, the same treatment MDM's TXA-17 already gets), left unset only when none did; otherwise `.outcome = "complete"`, the common-case default matching `Immunization.status`'s own "default to the most common real value" precedent. `.request` (FHIR-required) has no real originating `CoverageEligibilityRequest` resource to point at, since this converter processes a standalone 271 with no linked 270 Bundle - built as `Reference(identifier=Identifier(system=BHT_REFERENCE_SYSTEM, value=bht03))` rather than a resolvable `urn:uuid`, a disclosed, deliberate use of FHIR's own reference-by-identifier shape for exactly this "the referenced resource isn't actually in this Bundle" situation, not a workaround.
- **`app/edi/claim_status.py`** — Phase 2. `Edi276Builder`/`Edi277Builder` (Health Care Claim Status Request/Response) → FHIR `Task`, **one `Base*Builder` + subclass pair, not two sibling files like 270/271** - the deciding factor is that 276 and 277 target the identical FHIR resource and differ by exactly one toggle (whether `STC`/`businessStatus` is populated), whereas 270/271 target genuinely different resources; `_BaseClaimStatusBuilder.build_bundle()` does the shared walk, `Edi276Builder`/`Edi277Builder` just set `include_status = False`/`True`, the same ADT/SIU-style `Base*`+subclass shape used elsewhere in this app for "identical structure, one field differs" cases. Loop shape (`resolve_claim_status_loops`, public and reused by `validation.py` - see below) is a **5-level HL chain, one level deeper than 270/271's**, with its own HL03 code table verified against a real 276 segment example rather than assumed to carry over from 270/271 (HL03's numeric-code *meaning* is defined per-TR3, not universal - confirmed directly, not assumed, since an earlier general search suggested 837 uses `"20"` for Billing Provider, a different concept than 270/271's `"20"` Information Source): 2000A (`"20"`) payer, 2000B (`"21"`) information receiver, 2000C (`"19"`) **provider - a loop 270/271 doesn't have**, 2000D (`"22"`) subscriber, 2000E (`"23"`) dependent. **A genuinely different precedence rule from 270/271's**: rather than one "the patient" per transaction (dependent wins when present), a 276/277 can carry claim-status entries for *both* the subscriber and a dependent in one transaction set, each with its own repeating `TRN`-led claim-status group (`group_by_leader(members, "TRN", ["REF", "STC", "DTP", "SVC"])`) - so this builder walks both 2000D and 2000E when present, building one `Task` per `TRN` group, referencing whichever patient that group's own loop belongs to. `STC01` is the first composite element this app has needed to parse (`STC*F1:1:PR*...` - category:status:entity, colon-separated) - parsing it requires the interchange's own component separator, which is why `EdiTransactionBuilder.build_bundle()` gained a `delimiters` parameter this phase (see the `base.py`/`registry.py` bullet above). **`Task` as the target resource is a disclosed judgment call, not a guess**: confirmed directly against `hl7.org/fhir/R4/claim.html` that `Claim.use` is a closed `claim|preauthorization|predetermination` set with no status-inquiry concept, and confirmed against `hl7.org/fhir/R4/task.html` that `Task.businessStatus` is an unbound `CodeableConcept` explicitly meant for "business-specific nuances of the business state" - exactly STC's category/status pair. `STC_CATEGORY_PREFIX_TO_TASK_STATUS` (public, reused by `validation.py`) maps STC01-1's leading letter (verified against `x12.org/codes/claim-status-category-codes`: A=Acknowledgement→`received`, P=Pending→`in-progress`, F=Finalized→`completed`, R=Requires info→`on-hold`, E=Error→`failed`, D=Data search unsuccessful→`failed`) to `Task.status` - a **prefix-level**, not per-exact-code, crosswalk (X12's own list has finer distinctions within a prefix, e.g. A1 Received vs. A3 Rejected, that FHIR's fixed `task-status` value set has no room for without guessing); unrecognized/absent defaults to `"completed"`, the same "most common real value" fallback precedent as Medications' `moodCode`. **Two more `fhir.resources` required-but-not-`is_required()` surprises, confirmed by direct construction**: `Task.status` and `Task.intent` are both eagerly required despite `model_fields` reporting zero required fields - `intent="order"` for every Task this builder produces (a claim-status check is fundamentally a tracked order/request). `Task.for` is exposed as the Python attribute `for_fhir` (reserved word, same pattern as `Encounter.class_fhir`). `TRN02` (trace number) → `Task.identifier` (not `.input` - it identifies the Task itself, not a parameter fed into it). Disclosed scope limits, decided up front: only `STC01` is read (not the additional `STC10`/`STC11` composites, used when more than one status applies at once); any `STC` found within a `TRN` group is treated as claim-level status without distinguishing a further nested `SVC`-led service-line-level sub-group's own `STC`; `REF` is captured by the leader/member walk but not yet mapped to any FHIR field, the same treatment 270/271 already give it. **`resolve_claim_status_loops()` returns a `ResolvedClaimStatusLoops` dataclass (not a bare tuple), with `payer_nm1`/`receiver_nm1`/`provider_nm1`/`subscriber_nm1` all guaranteed resolved** (the function raises `MissingSegmentError` itself if any is absent, rather than deferring that check to `build_bundle`) - mirroring `resolve_eligibility_parties`' identical "raise here, not in the caller" discipline exactly. This was **not** the original shape: a follow-up code review caught that an earlier version only resolved the *loop objects*, leaving NM1-presence checks inline in `build_bundle` - which meant `validation.py`'s own rules (which only had the loop, not the NM1-checked guarantee) could see a subscriber loop with an absent NM1 and report a misleading "will still build a Patient, just with no HumanName" finding for a message that actually raises `MissingSegmentError` and converts to nothing. The **dependent loop gets the equivalent gate** `resolve_eligibility_parties` already applies to 270/271's dependent loop - `dependent_loop` is `None` whenever no 2000E exists *or* it exists but has no resolvable NM1 - since `build_bundle` only builds a Task for the dependent when its NM1 resolves, and an earlier version left this loop-existence-only in `resolve_claim_status_loops`, letting `validation.py`'s STC-reading rules see (and report on) claim-status content from a dependent loop the real builder silently drops entirely. See `tests/test_claim_status_mapping.py::test_dependent_loop_without_nm1_is_silently_dropped_not_reported` and `tests/test_claim_status_validation.py::test_dependent_loop_without_nm1_stc_is_not_reported` for the paired regression tests proving both sides now agree - the same "conversion and validation must never see a different segment set" discipline the 270/271 code review already established, applied here from the design stage rather than rediscovered after shipping.
- **`app/edi/prior_auth.py`** — Phase 3. `Edi278Builder` (Health Care Services Review - Request for Review and Response) → FHIR `Claim` (`use="preauthorization"`) always, plus a `ClaimResponse` when the message is a certification decision. **The one EDI family in this app where request and response share a single `ST01`**: confirmed by fetching real X12.org-published examples directly (`x12.org/examples/005010x217/example-04-request-home-health-care` for a request, `.../example-1b-response-request-review` for a response) and quoting their raw segment text verbatim rather than trusting a summarized secondary source (an initial AI-summarized reference page disagreed with the raw example on the deeper loops' HL03 codes - resolved by trusting the directly-fetched, verbatim-quoted primary source) - both examples literally begin `ST*278*...~`, distinguished only by `BHT02` (`"13"`=request, `"11"`=response). Because of this, `Edi278Builder` is a **single class registered once** under `"278"` in `registry.py` - unlike every other EDI pair, there is no second `ST01` to register a second builder under; `build_bundle()` reads `BHT02` itself to decide whether to also build a `ClaimResponse`. Loop shape: 2000A(`"20"`)→payer, 2000B(`"21"`)→requester, 2000C(`"22"`)→subscriber, 2000D(`"23"`)→dependent (optional) - **identical HL03 codes to 270/271's own table, confirmed genuinely, not coincidentally**, so `resolve_prior_auth_loops()` reuses `common.py`'s public `HL_INFORMATION_SOURCE`/`HL_INFORMATION_RECEIVER`/`HL_SUBSCRIBER`/`HL_DEPENDENT` constants directly rather than re-declaring a fourth copy - then continues one level deeper than `resolve_eligibility_parties` ever needs to, into 2000E(`"EV"`, Patient Event - a **child of whichever loop is "the patient"**, subscriber or dependent, carrying `UM`/`HI`/`DTP`/`HCR`), which is why this module has its own resolver (returning a `ResolvedPriorAuthLoops` dataclass, NM1-gated exactly like `ResolvedClaimStatusLoops` from the start - this project's second time hitting that exact discipline, applied proactively here rather than needing a third code-review catch) instead of extending `resolve_eligibility_parties`' own return shape. `UM03` (Service Type Code) reuses `common.py::build_service_type_category` directly - the identical X12 external code list 270's `EQ01`/271's `EB03` already use. `HI` (diagnosis codes) is the first *repeating* composite this app parses - up to 12 diagnosis composites positionally within one `HI` segment (`HI*BF:1831*BF:2630~` = two diagnoses, not two segments), each read via `component()` the same way `STC01` established. **`Claim`/`ClaimResponse` as the target resources are verified against the strongest source of any EDI phase in this app**: `Claim.use`/`Task` status-inquiry gap already confirmed for 276/277 ruled out `ClaimResponse`-only for status tracking, but 278 IS a real preauthorization request/response, and HL7's own Da Vinci PAS IG (`hl7.org/fhir/us/davinci-pas`, ballot-published, free) explicitly confirms `Claim`+`ClaimResponse` as its own target shape for X12 278 - the only phase in this app's EDI work with an actual official FHIR IG behind the target-resource choice, even though the IG doesn't publish the X12-segment-level crosswalk itself. **`HCR01` (Action Code) → `ClaimResponse.outcome`** via `HCR01_TO_OUTCOME` (public, reused by `validation.py`): `"A1"` Certified in Total→`"complete"`, `"A2"` Certified-Partial→`"partial"`, `"A3"` Not Certified→`"complete"` (a denial is still a completed decision, not a FHIR "error"), `"A4"` Pended→`"queued"` - verified against a real X12.org response example plus a published X12 RFI answer, since no single authoritative free HCR01 code table was found the way STC's Claim Status Category Codes are; unrecognized/absent defaults to `"complete"`. `HCR02` → `ClaimResponse.preAuthRef` (a plain string field, confirmed via `model_fields`) - the real authorization number (e.g. `"AUTH0001"`), not a reference. **A cleaner reference pattern than 271's**: since a 278 response message itself restates the original `UM`/`HI` review-request data (confirmed in the real X12.org response example), this builder materializes a real `Claim` from that response too and points `ClaimResponse.request` at it via a resolvable `urn:uuid` - unlike 271, which had no real `CoverageEligibilityRequest` to reference and fell back to `Reference(identifier=...)`. Disclosed scope limits, decided up front: the `2000F` Service-level sub-loop (per-service-line `UM`/`HCR`/`HI`) isn't modeled, mirroring `claim_status.py`'s identical `SVC`-nested-`STC` limit; `UM01` (Request Category Code) has no clean target in base FHIR `Claim.type` (bound to a fixed institutional|oral|pharmacy|professional|vision set UM01 doesn't map to) and is left unmapped, with `Claim.type` defaulting to `"professional"` and `Claim.priority` to `"normal"` (both disclosed "no fitting source field, default to the most common real value" judgment calls, the same category as 270's `DEFAULT_PURPOSE`); `HCR03` (Reason Code, X12 code source 886) is carried as a second disclosed-local-system adjudication entry rather than dropped, since X12 names that code source even though this app doesn't have a verified free copy of its contents. **Four issues a follow-up code review caught before commit**: (1) `ClaimInsurance.coverage`/`ClaimResponseInsurance.coverage` originally referenced the payer `Organization` directly instead of a real `Coverage` resource (confirmed wrong via `ClaimInsurance.coverage`'s own field description - "Reference to the insurance card level information contained in the Coverage resource") - `_build_coverage()` now builds one real `Coverage` per transaction, mirroring `eligibility_270.py`'s own construction exactly, which also fixed a second symptom of the same gap (a dependent's subscriber `Patient` going completely unreferenced by anything in the Bundle, since only `Coverage.subscriber` links it). (2) `HCR01` was read without `.strip().upper()` normalization, unlike every other coded lookup in this package (`NM108`, `EB01`) - a lowercase action code silently fell through to the `"complete"` default instead of resolving correctly (e.g. `"a4"` should resolve to `"queued"`, not `"complete"`) and simultaneously fired a spurious `edi.278-unrecognized-hcr-action-code` finding for a code that was actually recognized; fixed in both `prior_auth.py` and the matching `validation.py` rule. (3) The `HI` composite qualifier (`"BF"` = ICD-10-CM, the dominant real-world value) was always carried under a disclosed local placeholder system even though a real FHIR-canonical system exists for it (`http://hl7.org/fhir/sid/icd-10-cm`) - `_HI_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` now maps recognized qualifiers to their real canonical system, the same "canonical when recognized, local placeholder otherwise" pattern `NM1_ID_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` already established. (4) `BHT04`/`BHT05` were parsed and `MissingSegmentError`-checked twice for the same `BHT` segment - once in `_build_claim`, again in `_build_claim_response` - duplicating both the computation and the drift risk if one copy's logic were ever tweaked without the other; `_build_claim_response` now reuses `claim.created` directly. See `tests/test_prior_auth_mapping.py::test_lowercase_hcr_action_code_still_resolves_correct_outcome` and the `Coverage`-wiring assertions added to the existing fixture tests for the regression coverage.
- **`app/edi/remittance_835.py`** — Phase 4. `Edi835Builder` (Health Care Claim Payment/Advice - Electronic Remittance Advice) → FHIR `PaymentReconciliation`. **The one EDI family in this app with no `HL` hierarchy and no `BHT` segment at all** - confirmed directly (not assumed): the 005010X221 TR3's own segment sequence is `ST/BPR/TRN/[REF/DTM]/N1(payer)/N1(payee)/[LX]/CLP.../SE`, an `N1`-led header pair followed by a flat, repeating `CLP`-leader/`CAS`-member structure, with no `HL` segment anywhere - so `resolve_prior_auth_loops`-style loop-hierarchy resolution has no equivalent here; `group_by_leader` alone (for `CLP`→`CAS`/`SVC`/etc. member association) is the only grouping primitive this module needs. **`Bundle.identifier`/`.timestamp` can't come from `BHT03`/`04`/`05` the way `common.py::assemble_bundle` gets them for every other EDI family, since there is no `BHT` segment** - this module has its own `_assemble_835_bundle()` instead, using `TRN02` (the trace number, whose own stated purpose - "aid in reassociating payments and remittances" - is a natural fit) for `Bundle.identifier`, and deliberately leaving `Bundle.timestamp` **unset** rather than guessed at: no field in this transaction set carries a full date+time value the way `BHT04`+`05` do elsewhere, and `BPR16` (Payment Effective Date) is date-only - feeding it to `Bundle.timestamp` (a FHIR `instant`, no date-only form) would reproduce the exact bug class already disclosed for CDA's and this app's own `assemble_bundle` functions, so it's disclosed and skipped here rather than rediscovered as a bug. `N1`'s own shape is genuinely simpler than `NM1`'s (no first/last name split - just `N102` free-form name + `N103`/`N104` id qualifier/value) - `_build_organization_from_n1` is its own function, but reuses `common.py`'s now-public `NM1_ID_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` table directly for the id-qualifier lookup, since X12's own Identification Code Qualifier list (element 66) is the identical code list `NM108` already uses - only the segment *position* differs, not the code list. `BPR02` → `PaymentReconciliation.paymentAmount`, `BPR16` → both `.paymentDate` (its native, correct target - `.paymentDate` is a plain FHIR `date`, so this is a direct, safe mapping) and `.created` (a `dateTime`, which - unlike `Bundle.timestamp`'s `instant` - does have a date-only form, confirmed by inspecting `model_fields` directly, so reusing the same date-only `BPR16` value here is safe even though reusing it for `Bundle.timestamp` would not be). One `PaymentReconciliationDetail` per `CLP` group - verified by direct fetch of `hl7.org/fhir/R4/paymentreconciliation.html` that `.detail[]` itself operates at the claim/payable level ("correlates a payment amount to the adjudicated claim amounts"), not service-line level, so this module's own disclosed scope limit (`2110`/`SVC` service-line detail and itemized `CAS` adjustment-reason codes are captured by the leader/member walk but not individually mapped to any FHIR field - only `CLP04`'s aggregate paid amount per claim) matches the resource's own documented granularity, not an arbitrary cut. `.type` carries `CLP02` (claim status code) as a disclosed local-system coding (no single free, authoritative `CLP02` code table was found the way STC's Claim Status Category Codes are published on x12.org); `.identifier` carries `CLP01` (the claim submitter's own identifier); `.amount` carries `CLP04`. `.request`/`.response` (References to `Claim`/`ClaimResponse`) are left unset - a standalone 835 has no real `Claim`/`ClaimResponse` resource in its own Bundle to point at, the same gap 271 had for its own `.request` field, but without an identifier-based fallback this time since `PaymentReconciliationDetail.identifier` is already spoken for by `CLP01`. `.paymentIssuer` (payer) and `.requestor` (payee) are both disclosed field-selection choices, not obvious defaults: `.paymentIssuer` ("the party who generated the payment") is an unambiguous fit for the payer, but `.requestor` ("the practitioner who is responsible for the services rendered") is a looser fit for the payee - the closest available `PaymentReconciliation` field for "the entity associated with the services this payment covers" among the resource's own fields, chosen deliberately rather than left unset. `.outcome` is deliberately left unset - 835 has no source field indicating whether the remittance transaction itself processed successfully (unlike 271/278's `.outcome`, each sourced from a real field, `AAA`/`HCR` respectively). **`TRN` is a required segment, not merely read opportunistically**: the 005010X221 TR3 itself requires it ("used to uniquely identify this transaction set and to aid in reassociating payments and remittances"), and it's also this builder's only source for `Bundle.identifier` - `Edi835Builder` raises `MissingSegmentError` if `TRN` itself is absent or `TRN02` doesn't resolve, the same "require the segment/field this builder structurally depends on" precedent every other required-header-segment check in this app follows. **Two issues a follow-up code review caught before commit**: (1) `BPR02`/`BPR16` failing to resolve (the segment is present, just its value doesn't parse) originally raised `MissingSegmentError`, inconsistent with every sibling builder's own "segment present, value doesn't resolve → `MappingError`" precedent (`eligibility_270.py`'s `BHT04`-unresolvable check, `prior_auth.py`'s discharge-time-equivalent checks) - fixed to raise `MappingError` instead, see `tests/test_remittance_mapping.py::test_bpr02_unresolvable_raises_mapping_error_not_missing_segment_error`/`test_bpr16_unresolvable_raises_mapping_error_not_missing_segment_error`. (2) The `CLP`-scanning that feeds `.detail[]` originally routed through `group_by_leader(segments, "CLP", ["CAS", "NM1", "REF", "DTM", "AMT", "SVC"])` even though none of those member segments were ever read back out - simplified to a direct `[seg for seg in segments if seg[0] == "CLP"]`, since `group_by_leader`'s whole purpose (associating members with their leader) wasn't actually being used here.
- **`app/edi/claim_837p.py`** — Phase 5. `Edi837pBuilder` (Health Care Claim: Professional, 005010X222A2) → FHIR `Claim` (`use="claim"`, `type="professional"`), the first of three 837 variants this app ships - 837I followed as Phase 6 and 837D as Phase 7 (see each module's own bullet below), the same "one thing per slice" precedent as CCD before Discharge Summary. **The deepest, most cross-referential loop shape of any EDI phase in this app**, verified against a real X12.org-published example fetched and quoted verbatim (`x12.org/examples/005010x222/example-3a-claim-billing-provider-payer`) rather than trusted from an AI-summarized secondary source - the same discipline that already caught one wrong secondary-source claim for 278's own HL03 table caught a second one here: Stedi's own summarized reference page claimed 2010BB (payer) uses `NM1*41`, which the real example disproves - `NM1*41` is the 1000A Submitter loop (an interchange-level loop this app doesn't materialize), the real payer loop is `NM1*PR`. Loop shape - **a three-level HL chain, not 270/271/278's four**, and the numeric HL03 codes' *meaning* differs again despite two of them numerically coinciding with 270/271/278's own table (confirmed genuinely, not assumed - the same "don't assume a numeric HL03 code carries the same meaning across TR3s" discipline `claim_status.py` already established): 2000A(`"20"`, **Billing Provider**, not "Information Source")→`NM1*85`→billing provider Org/Practitioner, 2000B(`"22"`, Subscriber)→`NM1*IL`+`DMG`→subscriber `Patient` (this loop's own members ALSO carry `NM1*PR`, the payer - a genuine structural difference from every sibling family, where the payer gets its own root HL loop), 2000C(`"23"`, Patient, optional)→`NM1*QC`+`DMG`→patient `Patient`. **The 2300 Claim Information loop and everything nested under it (`HI`, the 2310B rendering-provider `NM1*82`, and repeating 2400 service lines) are NOT their own HL level** - unlike every other EDI family here, X12's `HL` segments stop at the patient level for 837P, so `CLM`/`HI`/`NM1*82`/`LX`/`SV1`/`DTP` are all flat members of whichever loop is "the patient" (`claim_loop` in `Resolved837pLoops` - the 2000C loop when present, else 2000B itself). **`claim_loop` and patient-resource-construction precedence are deliberately gated independently, unlike every other EDI family's dependent-loop resolver**: `claim_loop` resolves purely structurally (the 2000C loop whenever `HL03="23"` was emitted at all, regardless of whether its own NM1 resolves) since `CLM`/`HI`/service lines are physically nested wherever that HL loop's members actually are - X12 doesn't re-attribute them to 2000B just because 2000C's NM1 happens to be malformed, so this resolver can't either; only `patient_is_dependent` (whether `Claim.patient`/`Coverage.beneficiary` point at the 2000C patient vs. falling back to the subscriber) uses the "no NM1 → treat as absent" gate every sibling family's own dependent loop already does. Getting this wrong (reusing 270/271/278's single combined gate, which was the first version written) makes a malformed-but-present 2000C loop's own `CLM` invisible to `build_bundle()`, which then raises `MissingSegmentError` for a claim that's actually right there - caught before commit by `tests/test_claim_837p_mapping.py::test_dependent_loop_without_nm1_falls_back_to_subscriber`, not by a later code review. `CLM05-1` (Facility Code Value / Place of Service) maps to the real, verified CMS canonical CodeSystem (`https://www.cms.gov/Medicare/Coding/place-of-service-codes/Place_of_Service_Code_Set`, confirmed by direct fetch - unlike most of this app's disclosed local-system fallbacks) applied as a claim-wide default `Claim.item[].locationCodeableConcept` for every service line, since `SV1-05` (the situational per-line override) is out of scope this phase. `SV1-01`'s procedure-code qualifier `"HC"` is a **genuinely unresolvable ambiguity, confirmed directly rather than assumed**: X12's own "HC" qualifier covers both CPT (AMA-owned) and HCPCS Level II (CMS-owned) with no way to tell which from the X12 element alone - a real, open FHIR/X12 interop gap (even the Da Vinci PAS IG's own JIRA tracker has an open issue about this exact ambiguity for the sibling 278 transaction) - so it stays on a disclosed local placeholder rather than being forced into either canonical system and being wrong roughly half the time. `SV1-07` (Composite Diagnosis Code Pointer, up to 4 1-based positions into `HI`'s own diagnosis order) resolves against `Claim.diagnosis[]`'s own sequence numbers in the identical left-to-right order `build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts` returns them in; a pointer that doesn't resolve (out of range, non-numeric) is silently skipped rather than raising, surfaced separately as `edi.837p-diagnosis-pointer-unresolved` in validation. `NM1*82` (2310B Rendering Provider, optional) materializes as a real `Practitioner`/`Organization` and is referenced via `Claim.careTeam[]` with `role="primary"` (confirmed against `hl7.org/fhir/R4/valueset-claim-careteamrole.html`'s own 4-code value set) - distinct from `Claim.provider` (the *billing* provider, per that field's own FHIR definition: "the provider... responsible for the claim"), with every `Claim.item[]` this module builds carrying `careTeamSequence=[1]` back to it when present. Disclosed Phase-5 scope limits, decided up front (837 files are commonly batched the same way every earlier EDI phase's own "first ST/SE transaction set only" limit already discloses, extended one level further here): only the first 2300 claim is mapped; 2010AB Pay-to Provider, 2310C Service Facility Location, and the 2320/2330 Other Subscriber Information (COB) loops are never read; `CLM03`/`CLM04`/`CLM06`-`CLM11` beyond `CLM05` have no clean target in base FHIR `Claim` and are left unmapped, the same "no fitting field, disclosed and skipped" treatment 278's `UM01` already gets. **Three issues a follow-up code review caught before commit**: (1) the service-line `DTP` lookup originally took "the first `DTP` in the 2400 group" regardless of `DTP01` (Date/Time Qualifier) - a line carrying a differently-qualified `DTP` (e.g. `"463"` Prescription Date) ahead of the real `"472"` Service Date one would have silently mislabeled it as `servicedDate`; fixed via `find_dtp_by_qualifier(members, DTP_SERVICE_DATE)` (both public, reused by the matching `validation.py` rule so conversion and validation can't disagree about which `DTP` counts), the same "check the specific qualifier, don't just grab the first segment of this type" discipline `STC01`/TXA dedup already established elsewhere in this app. (2) `edi.837p-diagnosis-pointer-unresolved`'s own diagnosis count was originally re-implemented as a second, hand-rolled `HI`-composite-counting loop in `validation.py` instead of calling `build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts` directly - the same drift risk this exact promotion (see the `common.py` bullet above) was extracted specifically to eliminate; fixed to reuse `len(build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts(hi, delimiters))`. (3) `Claim.item[].sequence` was assigned via `enumerate()` over every `LX` group *before* filtering out groups with no `SV1`, so a malformed claim with a non-first empty `LX` group produced non-contiguous sequence numbers (e.g. `[1, 3]` instead of `[1, 2]`) - fixed by numbering only the items actually built (`len(items) + 1`). See `tests/test_claim_837p_mapping.py::test_wrongly_qualified_dtp_is_not_used_as_serviced_date`/`test_item_sequence_numbers_stay_contiguous_when_a_middle_line_has_no_sv1` and `tests/test_claim_837p_validation.py::test_837p_wrongly_qualified_future_dtp_does_not_trigger_service_date_rule` for the regression coverage.
- **`app/edi/claim_837i.py`** — Phase 6. `Edi837iBuilder` (Health Care Claim: Institutional, 005010X223A2) → FHIR `Claim` (`use="claim"`, `type="institutional"`). **Disclosed verification-source gap, restated per-module the same way every EDI phase discloses it**: X12's own TR3 for 837I is paywalled (no official free X12-to-FHIR IG exists for this transaction set) - the segment shape below is verified against a real X12.org-published example (`x12.org/examples/005010x223/example-1a-institutional-claim`, fetched and quoted verbatim) plus cross-referenced free companion guides (CMS/Palmetto/CGS) where the one example alone didn't label a field's exact position (e.g. `CL1`'s three elements); the FHIR-side CodeSystem claims (NUBC Revenue Codes, NUBC Patient Discharge Status, `ClaimInformationCategoryCodes`, `claimcareteamrole`) were each confirmed by direct fetch of `hl7.org`/`terminology.hl7.org`, not assumed. **The second real consumer of 837P's own 3-level HL chain, confirmed genuinely identical by the same real example, not assumed from the "both are 837" family resemblance**: 2000A(`"20"`, Billing Provider)→`NM1*85`, 2000B(`"22"`, Subscriber)→`NM1*IL`+`DMG` with the payer `NM1*PR` nested in the same loop's own members, 2000C(`"23"`, Patient, optional)→`NM1*QC`+`DMG` - byte-for-byte the same loop/NM1/payer-placement shape `claim_837p.py` already established. `claim_837i.py` has its own independent `resolve_837i_loops()` rather than importing `claim_837p.py`'s directly - deliberately: this codebase's "extract on second use" discipline means extracting *before* 837I's own test suite had independently proven every edge case (the `claim_loop`/`patient_is_dependent` two-gate split especially) would risk the same "assumed-identical, actually diverged" trap `build_coverage`/`build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts` were extracted specifically to eliminate - promoting a shared resolver to `common.py` remains a natural future step once genuinely proven, not a decision made preemptively here. **Where 837I genuinely diverges from 837P, confirmed by the same real example**: `CLM05-1` is a UB-04 Type of Bill code (what *kind* of stay this was), not a Place-of-Service code the way 837P's is - no verified free FHIR-canonical system was found for it (unlike 837P's real CMS POS system), so it's disclosed and left entirely unmapped rather than reused via `Claim.item.locationCodeableConcept` (which would be semantically wrong here). **`CL1` (Institutional Claim Code) has no 837P equivalent at all** - only `CL103` (Patient Status Code) is mapped this slice, via `Claim.supportingInfo` with `category` = the real `discharge` code from FHIR's own `ClaimInformationCategoryCodes` value set (confirmed by fetch: "Discharge status and discharge to locations," a direct semantic match) and `code` on the real NUBC Patient Discharge Status CodeSystem (`https://www.nubc.org/CodeSystem/PatDischargeStatus` - confirmed by fetch, including a dedicated HL7 Terminology Authority page explicitly naming CL103 as this code list's X12 source field); `CL101`/`CL102` (admission type/source) have no comparably-fitting category and are disclosed-and-unmapped. **`HI`'s institutional usage needed a real fix mid-implementation, not just a disclosed limitation**: institutional claims can carry *several* `HI` segments per claim, each dedicated to one code-list "type" (principal/other diagnosis, occurrence, value, condition) distinguished only by the qualifier of its own composites, not any segment-level flag - confirmed directly against the real example, which splits Principal Diagnosis (`HI*ABK:3669~`) and Other Diagnosis (`HI*ABF:4019*ABF:79431~`) into two separate physical `HI` segments. An initial version used `find_segment(..., "HI")` (grabbing only the first) the same way 837P does (837P never needs more, since professional claims' `HI` usage is diagnosis-only by TR3 definition) - caught immediately via direct testing against the real fixture, not a later review: fixed by `iter_diagnosis_hi_segments()` (public, reused by `claim_837i_validation.py`'s own missing-diagnosis rule), which walks every `HI` segment in the claim loop and includes only those whose first composite uses a recognized diagnosis qualifier (checked against `common.py::HI_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM`'s own keys) - occurrence/value/condition-coded `HI` segments (`BH`/`BE`/`BG` qualifiers, all real and present in the example) are structurally skipped rather than folded into `Claim.diagnosis[]` as bogus "unrecognized qualifier" entries, and are themselves disclosed-and-deferred (each would need its own `supportingInfo` category mapping decided independently - no clean fit was found in `ClaimInformationCategoryCodes` either - a large enough scope to be a future slice, not bundled here). **`SV2` (Institutional Service Line) replaces `SV1` - a related but not identical composite shape, confirmed by direct verification**: `SV2-01` (Revenue Code) is the required, primary field - `SV2-02` (the composite procedure identifier) is genuinely optional in real institutional lines (e.g. a bare room-and-board revenue-code line carries none at all), so `ClaimItem.productOrService` (still FHIR-required regardless, the same "don't trust `model_fields`" lesson every EDI phase has learned at least once) falls back to `CodeableConcept(text=f"Revenue code {revenue_code}")` rather than a generic placeholder, since the revenue code itself is always informative here. `SV2-01` maps to the real NUBC Revenue Codes CodeSystem (`https://www.nubc.org/CodeSystem/RevenueCodes` - confirmed by fetch) via `ClaimItem.revenue`, a field 837P's own `SV1`-derived items never populate. **`SV2` has no diagnosis-pointer composite at all, unlike `SV1-07`** - confirmed directly, not assumed: institutional diagnoses apply at the claim level via `HI`, not per-service-line, so `Claim.item[].diagnosisSequence` is never populated by this module - a genuine structural fact about 837I, not a missing feature carried over incompletely from 837P. **Attending Provider (`NM1*71`) replaces 837P's Rendering Provider (`NM1*82`)** as the one `2310`-level provider role this slice materializes, mapped to `Claim.careTeam[]` with `role="primary"` (the same closest-fit code 837P's own rendering-provider mapping already uses - no `"attending"` code exists in the 4-code `claimcareteamrole` value set either), with every `Claim.item[]` carrying `careTeamSequence=[1]` back to it when present - **a follow-up code review caught that the first version omitted this linkage entirely** (every other field-for-field-identical piece of the 837P shape had it), fixed by threading `care_team_sequence` through `_build_service_line_item()`, see `tests/test_claim_837i_mapping.py`'s own `careTeamSequence` assertions for the regression coverage on both the present and absent branches. Disclosed Phase-6 scope limits, decided up front, mirroring 837P's own disclosure style: only the first 2300 claim per transaction set is mapped; 2010AB Pay-to Provider, 2310B/C Operating/Other-Operating Physician, 2310E Service Facility Location, and the 2320/2330 Other Subscriber Information (COB) loops are never read; `CLM05-1`, `CL101`/`CL102`, and `HI`'s occurrence/value/condition-code usages have no mapped FHIR target this slice; `SV206`/`SV207` (non-covered charges) are not read.
- **`app/edi/claim_837d.py`** — Phase 7, the final "big five" HIPAA family. `Edi837dBuilder` (Health Care Claim: Dental, 005010X224A2) → FHIR `Claim` (`use="claim"`, `type="oral"`). Same disclosed verification-source gap as every EDI phase (X12's own TR3 is paywalled) - verified against a real X12.org-published example (`x12.org/examples/005010x224/example-01-commercial-health-insurance`, fetched and quoted verbatim) plus a cross-referenced Stedi X12 segment reference for `SV3`'s own element list; the FHIR-side CodeSystem claims (CDT, ADA Universal Tooth Designation System, ADA Tooth Surface Codes) were each confirmed by direct fetch of `terminology.hl7.org`. **The third real consumer of 837P's/837I's own 3-level HL chain, confirmed genuinely identical by the same real example, not assumed from the "another 837 variant" family resemblance**: 2000A(`"20"`, Billing Provider)→`NM1*85`, 2000B(`"22"`, Subscriber)→`NM1*IL`+`DMG` with the payer `NM1*PR` nested in the same loop's own members, 2000C(`"23"`, Patient, optional)→`NM1*QC`+`DMG`. `claim_837d.py` has its own independent `resolve_837d_loops()` rather than importing 837P's/837I's, the same "extract on second use, once genuinely proven, not preemptively" discipline `claim_837i.py` already established - a shared `resolve_837_loops()` in `common.py` remains a natural future step now that a third independently-tested implementation exists to actually prove the whole shape identical, not just the happy path one real example exercises. **Where 837D genuinely diverges from 837P/837I, confirmed by the same real example**: `CLM05-1` uses the **same** Place-of-Service vocabulary as 837P's (confirmed by `SV3-03`'s own field description, "Place of Service Codes for Professional **or Dental** Services") - not 837I's UB-04 Type of Bill - so this module reuses `common.py::POS_CODE_SYSTEM`/`build_place_of_service_from_clm05` directly, promoted there from `claim_837p.py` once 837D became a second real consumer. **`SV3` (Dental Service) replaces `SV1`/`SV2`**: `SV3-01`'s procedure-code qualifier is `"AD"` in the real example (ADA/CDT codes) - unlike 837P's genuinely ambiguous `"HC"` qualifier, dental claims overwhelmingly use exactly one code system in practice, so `"AD"` maps directly to the real, verified `http://www.ada.org/cdt` canonical system; any other qualifier falls back to a disclosed local system. `SV3-06` (Quantity) → `Claim.item.quantity`; `SV3-11` (Diagnosis Code Pointer) → `Claim.item.diagnosisSequence`, resolved the identical way 837P's `SV1-07` already is (up to 4 1-based positions, out-of-range/non-numeric pointers silently skipped) - its own composite sub-structure was not independently confirmed against a primary source (the one real example never populates it), inferred by analogy with `SV1-07`'s confirmed shape and disclosed as such. **`TOO` (Tooth Information) is its own segment, not part of `SV3` at all, but a member of the identical `LX` service-line group** - confirmed directly by the real example. `TOO02` (tooth number) → `Claim.item.bodySite`; `TOO03` (up to 5 colon-separated single-letter surface codes) → `Claim.item.subSite` (confirmed via direct construction that this field is genuinely a `list[CodeableConcept]`, unlike `bodySite`). `TOO01="JP"` (Universal/National system, the dominant real-world US system) maps to the real `http://terminology.hl7.org/CodeSystem/ADAUniversalToothDesignationSystem` - **deliberately NOT** FHIR's own `ex-tooth`/`tooth` ValueSet's backing CodeSystem, which is FDI (international) numbering, a genuinely different vocabulary for the same physical tooth (confirmed by direct fetch, the same trap CDA's `F`/`M`/`UN` gender codes already taught this app to check for); `TOO03`'s surface letters map to the real `http://terminology.hl7.org/CodeSystem/ADAToothSurfaceCodes`, confirmed to use the identical letters by direct fetch rather than assumed from the similarly-named international `FDI-surface` CodeSystem. **`DTP*472` (Service Date) commonly appears at the CLAIM level (2300) for dental claims, not per-service-line (2400) the way 837P's/837I's own service lines carry it** - confirmed directly by the real example, whose two service lines carry no `DTP` of their own but one claim-level `DTP*472` does. `resolve_line_dtp_raw_date()` (public, shared with `claim_837d_validation.py`) resolves `Claim.item.servicedDate` per line from a per-line `DTP*472` when present, falling back to the one claim-level `DTP*472` otherwise - scoped via `find_claim_level_segments()` (segments before the first `LX` only) rather than a naive whole-claim-loop scan, since `claim_loop.member_segments` is one flat list spanning both 2300 and every repeated 2400 group and `DTP*472` legitimately appears at both levels; a naive scan could silently grab a per-line `DTP` and misapply it as the claim-wide default. Rendering Provider uses `NM1*82` - the same code as 837P's, **not** 837I's `NM1*71` Attending Provider - confirmed directly by the real example, mapped to `Claim.careTeam[]` with `role="primary"` and `careTeamSequence=[1]` on every `Claim.item[]` when present, built correctly from the start this time (a follow-up code review caught this exact linkage missing once already, for 837I). Disclosed Phase-7 scope limits, decided up front, mirroring 837P's/837I's own disclosure style: only the first 2300 claim per transaction set is mapped; 2010AB Pay-to Provider, 2310C Service Facility Location, and the 2320/2330 Other Subscriber Information (COB) loops are never read; `SV3-03`'s own per-line place-of-service override, `SV3-04` (Oral Cavity Designation), `SV3-05` (Prosthesis/Crown/Inlay Code), and `SV3-07` through `SV3-10` (free-text reason, copay status, provider agreement, predetermination indicator) have no mapped FHIR target this slice.
- **837P, 837I, and 837D share the literal `ST01="837"` with no `BHT02`-style field inside the transaction set to distinguish them** (unlike 278's own request/response split, which does have `BHT02`) - a real dispatch problem `claim_837p.py`'s own Phase 5 bullet flagged but didn't yet resolve, surfaced again once Phase 6 needed an actual answer.
