all with SE01 counts computed and verified programmatically, same as every other EDI fixture. `tests/test_prior_auth_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite: the request fixture proves zero `ClaimResponse` is produced (BHT02="13"), the response fixtures prove `ClaimResponse.request` resolves to the real materialized `Claim` via `urn:uuid` (not a by-identifier fallback like 271's, since 278 responses restate their own request data), `HCR01`→`.outcome` resolves for both a recognized certified code and the `"complete"` fallback for an unrecognized one, and `Claim.item[].productOrService` is asserted in both its coded (`UM03` populated) and text-only (`UM03` empty, matching the real example fixture) shapes - a mistake in the test itself (assuming the real X12.org example's own `UM03` was populated when it verifiably wasn't) was caught by simply running the test against the real fixture rather than trusting the assertion on sight. `tests/test_generate_prior_auth.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 278-specific additions: `test_response_hcr_presence_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving `_build_claim_response()`'s "no HCR → no ClaimResponse" branch is actually exercised, not just theoretically reachable), `test_request_never_carries_hcr`, and `test_response_hcr_action_code_varies_across_recognized_and_unrecognized` (mirroring 277's equivalent STC-category coverage proof). `tests/test_prior_auth_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape, including `edi.278-response-missing-hcr` (asserted absent for a *request*, not just present for a response missing its HCR, to prove the rule's own BHT02 gate isn't accidentally unconditional) and the `edi.would-not-convert` case specific to a missing 2000E Patient Event loop - a failure mode no earlier EDI phase has, since none of them nest a loop this way.

**Phase 4 (835) testing** follows the same `test_remittance_*.py` own-file-per-family pattern. `tests/fixtures/edi_835_basic.x12` is built from a real, internally-consistent raw 835 example (charge/paid/patient-responsibility amounts that actually sum correctly, `BPR02` matching the claim's own paid amount) with every field position independently verified against X12 segment references before trusting it, not just copied wholesale; `edi_835_multi_claim.x12` covers two claims (one paid, one denied, `CLP02="4"`); `edi_835_missing_bpr.x12` covers the `MissingSegmentError` path. `tests/test_remittance_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite: asserts `Bundle.identifier` resolves from `TRN02` (not a `BHT`-derived field, since none exists), one `PaymentReconciliationDetail` per claim with the correct `CLP01`/`CLP02`/`CLP04` mapping, and the `N1`-shaped identifier builder's reuse of `common.py`'s now-public `NM1_ID_QUALIFIER_SYSTEM` table (asserting the payee's `XX`-qualified id resolves to the real NPI canonical system, not a local placeholder) - plus, added during the follow-up code review, `test_missing_trn_raises_missing_segment_error` and the two `MappingError`-not-`MissingSegmentError` regression tests for `BPR02`/`BPR16` unresolvable-but-present values (see `remittance_835.py`'s own bullet above for why these were wrong the first time). `tests/test_generate_remittance.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 835-specific additions: `test_bpr02_equals_sum_of_clp04_paid_amounts` (direct proof that the generator's "build claims first, sum into the header" discipline actually holds across seeds, not just by construction) and `test_cas_presence_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving the `CLP`→`CAS` leader/member association is exercised in both directions). `tests/test_remittance_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape; its `_build()` helper composes a flat segment list directly (no HL-hierarchy skeleton to build, unlike every other EDI validation test file's helper), reflecting 835's own genuinely flatter structure - plus, added during the same review, `test_835_non_finite_clp_amounts_do_not_crash_validation` (a `CLP03`/`CLP04` value spelled `"NaN"`/`"Infinity"` must not crash `edi.835-claim-paid-exceeds-charge`/`edi.835-bpr02-total-mismatch`, the regression proof for `parse_decimal`'s `.is_finite()` guard), `test_835_bpr16_in_future_is_warning`, and `test_835_missing_payer_name_is_info`.

**Phase 5 (837P) testing** follows the same `test_claim_837p_*.py` own-file-per-family pattern. `tests/fixtures/edi_837p_basic.x12` is adapted directly from the real X12.org example this phase's own module docstring cites (trimmed to this phase's actual scope - no pay-to provider, no COB) with a subscriber-is-the-patient shape and two service lines, one of which deliberately carries a resolvable multi-position diagnosis pointer (`SV1-07="1:2"`); `edi_837p_with_dependent.x12` covers the 2000C patient-loop precedence case (dependent ≠ subscriber) with an organization billing provider and no rendering provider; `edi_837p_missing_bht.x12` covers the `MissingSegmentError` path - all three built programmatically with SE01 counts computed and verified via `parse_interchange()` before saving, same as every other EDI fixture. `tests/test_claim_837p_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite, plus several inline-built (non-fixture) cases for shapes the two fixtures don't cover: `CLM` absent, `CLM02`/`BHT04` present-but-unresolvable (`MappingError`, not `MissingSegmentError` - matching every sibling builder's own precedent), a `2000C` loop present but missing its own `NM1*QC` (proving `claim_loop` still finds `CLM` structurally while `Claim.patient` correctly falls back to the subscriber - the regression test for `Resolved837pLoops`' own two-independent-gates design, written proactively rather than only after a review caught the alternative), an out-of-range `SV1-07` pointer, and a service line with `LX` but no `SV1`. `tests/test_generate_claim_837p.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 837P-specific additions: `test_rendering_provider_presence_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving `Claim.careTeam`'s present/absent branch) and `test_diagnosis_pointer_out_of_range_occurs_and_is_the_minority` (fuzz-proving `edi.837p-diagnosis-pointer-unresolved`'s own deliberate ~10% fuzz is actually exercised, mirroring 277's/278's own recognized-vs-unrecognized-code coverage proofs). `tests/test_claim_837p_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape. A follow-up code review (run before commit, same as every other phase) caught three issues, each with its own regression test: a service-line `DTP` lookup that ignored `DTP01` (fixed via the now-public `find_dtp_by_qualifier`/`DTP_SERVICE_DATE`, shared between `claim_837p.py` and its matching `validation.py` rule so both sides read the identical DTP - see `test_wrongly_qualified_dtp_is_not_used_as_serviced_date`/`test_837p_wrongly_qualified_future_dtp_does_not_trigger_service_date_rule`); `edi.837p-diagnosis-pointer-unresolved` re-implementing the `HI`-counting loop instead of reusing `build_diagnosis_codeable_concepts` (fixed to call it directly - the exact duplication risk that promotion was extracted to eliminate); and non-contiguous `Claim.item[].sequence` numbers when a malformed middle service line has no `SV1` (fixed by numbering only the items actually built - see `test_item_sequence_numbers_stay_contiguous_when_a_middle_line_has_no_sv1`). The same pre-existing `"BF"`-qualifier bug fix documented in the `common.py`/`prior_auth.py` bullets above has its own direct unit tests in `tests/test_edi_common.py`, independent of any fixture.

**Phase 6 (837I) testing** follows the same `test_claim_837i_*.py` own-file-per-family pattern. `tests/fixtures/edi_837i_basic.x12` is adapted directly from the real X12.org example this phase's own module docstring cites (trimmed to this app's disclosed scope - no COB, no pay-to provider) and deliberately keeps the example's own two-separate-`HI`-segments shape (Principal + Other diagnosis) rather than simplifying it away, since that shape is exactly what caught the `iter_diagnosis_hi_segments()` bug (see `claim_837i.py`'s own bullet); `edi_837i_with_dependent.x12` covers the 2000C patient-loop precedence case with a person billing provider, no attending provider, and a procedure-code-less revenue-code service line (`SV2*0120**250.00*DA*1~`); `edi_837i_missing_bht.x12` covers the `MissingSegmentError` path - all three built and verified the same programmatic way as every other EDI fixture. `tests/test_claim_837i_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite, plus several inline-built cases: `CLM` absent, `CLM02`/`BHT04` present-but-unresolvable (`MappingError`), a claim carrying `HI` segments for occurrence/value/condition codes alongside a real diagnosis one (proving none of the three pollute `Claim.diagnosis[]` - the direct regression test for the `iter_diagnosis_hi_segments()` fix), a `2000C` loop present but missing its own `NM1*QC` (the same two-independent-gates regression `claim_837p.py`'s own test already established, proactively written here too rather than re-derived from a second incident), and a service line with `LX` but no `SV2`. `tests/test_generate_claim_837i.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 837I-specific additions: `test_attending_provider_presence_varies_across_seeds`, `test_procedure_code_presence_on_service_lines_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving both branches of `ClaimItem.productOrService`'s coded-vs-revenue-code-text fallback), `test_occurrence_value_condition_hi_segments_occur_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving all three non-diagnosis `HI` qualifiers the filter must skip actually get generated, not just the diagnosis-qualified ones), and `test_generated_message_dispatches_to_837i_builder_via_st03` (an end-to-end proof, through the real `convert_edi_to_bundle` pipeline, that a generated 837I sample's `ST03` actually routes it to `Edi837iBuilder`, not `Edi837pBuilder` - the regression test for the `ST01="837"`-shared-by-both-variants dispatch problem this phase had to solve). `tests/test_claim_837i_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape, plus `test_837p_rules_do_not_fire_for_837i_transaction` (proving the `ST03`-based dispatch inside `validate_interchange` itself, not just the registry's own dispatch, correctly separates the two rule sets). `tests/test_edi_registry.py` gained direct unit tests for `get_transaction_builder`'s own `st01`+`st03` dispatch: professional/institutional resolution by `ST03`, and the disclosed default-to-professional behavior when `st03` is absent or unrecognized (`ST03` being situational, not required, per the TR3s). A follow-up code review caught two issues, each with its own regression test: `Claim.item[]` entries never carried `careTeamSequence` back to the attending-provider `careTeam` entry, unlike the identical 837P shape (fixed by threading `care_team_sequence` through `_build_service_line_item()` - see the `careTeamSequence` assertions added to `test_claim_837i_mapping.py`'s existing fixture tests); and `_build_hi_837i`'s own return-type annotation claimed `str` when it actually returns `list[str]` (harmless at runtime since callers already used `.extend()` correctly, but misleading to a reader or type checker - fixed to `list[str]`).

**Phase 7 (837D) testing** follows the same `test_claim_837d_*.py` own-file-per-family pattern, completing the full EDI suite's testing coverage. `tests/fixtures/edi_837d_basic.x12` is adapted directly from the real X12.org example this phase's own module docstring cites (trimmed to this app's disclosed scope) and deliberately keeps the example's own no-`HI`-segment shape (dental claims commonly carry no diagnosis at all) rather than adding one just to force "zero findings" parity with sibling families' basic fixtures - it legitimately produces one `edi.837d-missing-diagnosis` info finding, a disclosed design choice, not an inconsistency; `edi_837d_no_dependent.x12` covers the subscriber-is-the-patient shape with a resolvable diagnosis pointer and a per-line (not claim-level) `DTP*472`; `edi_837d_missing_bht.x12` covers the `MissingSegmentError` path - all three built the same programmatic way as every other EDI fixture, with the `SV3` segment specifically built via a positionally-indexed fields list rather than hand-counted asterisks after an initial hand-typed version put a diagnosis pointer at the wrong element position (caught immediately by parsing the fixture back and checking the field, not by a later review). `tests/test_claim_837d_mapping.py` is the field-assertion suite (13 tests), plus inline-built cases mirroring 837P's/837I's own precedents: `CLM` absent, `CLM02`/`BHT04` present-but-unresolvable (`MappingError`), a `2000C` loop present but missing its own `NM1*QC` (the same two-independent-gates regression `claim_837p.py`'s test already established), an out-of-range `SV3-11` diagnosis pointer, a service line with `LX` but no `SV3`, and an unrecognized procedure/tooth-number qualifier each falling back to its own disclosed local system. `tests/test_generate_claim_837d.py` mirrors the other EDI generator test files, with 837D-specific additions: `test_diagnosis_presence_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving `edi.837d-missing-diagnosis`'s own present/absent branch, the one family where "absent" is expected to be common rather than rare), `test_diagnosis_pointer_out_of_range_occurs_and_is_the_minority` (mirroring 837P's identical precedent for `SV3-11`), `test_tooth_information_presence_varies_across_seeds` (`TOO`'s own present/absent branch per line), and `test_service_date_source_varies_across_seeds` (fuzz-proving all three of `resolve_line_dtp_raw_date()`'s own outcomes - per-line DTP wins, claim-level DTP fallback, neither present - actually occur across seeds, the one family-specific date-resolution shape no sibling generator needs to exercise). `tests/test_claim_837d_validation.py` mirrors the other EDI validation test files' per-rule shape, plus `test_837d_line_level_dtp_takes_precedence_over_future_claim_level_dtp` (a dental-specific regression proving a line's own past-dated `DTP*472` suppresses the future-date warning even when the claim-level fallback `DTP*472` would itself be in the future - the precedence order, not just the fallback's existence) and `test_837p_and_837i_rules_do_not_fire_for_837d_transaction` (the third-family instance of the `ST03`-dispatch-isolation check `claim_837i_validation.py`'s own equivalent test already established). `tests/test_edi_registry.py` gained `test_get_transaction_builder_resolves_837_dental_by_st03`, alongside the existing professional/institutional-by-`ST03` tests. `tests/test_generator_registry.py`'s `_EXPECTED_TYPES` and `tests/test_routes.py`'s dropdown-label/parametrized-generate/fixture-based-convert assertions were extended the same mechanical way every earlier phase's own route-test additions were.

**Deduplication testing** (`tests/test_dedup.py`) is direct (non-fuzz) unit tests against small hand-built Bundles (mirroring every other pillar's own "don't reuse generator output for rule-specific tests" discipline) covering: identifier-based merge, name-fallback merge for all four resource types (`Practitioner`/`Organization`/`Location`, `Patient` implicitly covered by the same code path), no-merge on differing identifiers, no-merge when neither identifier nor name resolves, `("identifier", ...)`/`("name", ...)` key-tagging never letting an identifier-matched pair collide with a merely name-matched one, `Condition` (a representative clinical/event type) never deduplicated even with identical fields, cross-reference rewriting, the original Bundle never mutated, multiple independent duplicate groups merged correctly in one pass, and survivor entry order preserved. `test_real_837p_fixture_merges_billing_and_rendering_provider` runs the real motivating case end-to-end through the actual `convert_to_bundle()` pipeline (not a hand-built Bundle) against the existing `edi_837p_basic.x12` fixture, confirming both the practitioner count drops from two to one and `Claim.provider`/`Claim.careTeam[].provider` both correctly resolve to the kept resource. `tests/test_routes.py` covers the wiring: `deduplicate` omitted or `false` never adds a `deduplication` key to the JSON response (proving the feature is genuinely opt-in, not just default-off), `deduplicate: true` against a fixture with nothing to merge reports `resources_merged: 0` (not an error), the same 837P fixture merges through the full HTTP API, and the no-JS `/convert` form path (checkbox present as `"on"`) renders the summary line server-side. A full sweep of every registered `(message_type, trigger_event)` combination's generator output, run through `convert_to_bundle()` then `deduplicate_bundle()`, confirms the generic reference-rewriting walk never crashes against any resource shape this app produces - not committed as a standing test (it would just re-run every other pillar's own generator fuzz coverage a second time for no additional signal, since dedup's own crash surface is the generic walk, not per-mapper field shapes), but run manually before shipping this feature.

**Bidirectional-transformation testing** (`tests/test_transform.py`) covers the registry (`("HL7", "ADT", "A01")` is listed; an unregistered target raises `MappingError`; `get_builder` normalizes case/whitespace the same way `get_mapper`/`get_document_builder`/`get_transaction_builder` already do) and the `AdtA01Builder` itself: a full forward-then-reverse field-assertion test (every PID/PV1 field this builder populates checked against its exact expected value, not just "some text was produced"), a genuine round-trip proof (`test_round_trip_produces_a_convertible_message_again` feeds the generated text back through the real `convert_hl7_to_bundle()` and asserts the resulting Bundle's Patient/Encounter fields match the original - the test that would catch a builder producing syntactically-plausible-but-wrong HL7v2, which a bare "text starts with MSH" assertion never would), a missing-Patient case (`MappingError`), an Encounter-optional case (mirrors the forward direction's own "Encounter may be absent" tolerance), and a direct regression test for the PID-5 given-name-splitting fix (`test_multiple_given_names_map_to_separate_xpn_components` - an earlier version space-joined `Patient.name[0].given` into one XPN component instead of two, silently collapsing a first-name/middle-name distinction the forward mapper had correctly kept separate; caught by re-running the round-trip test and noticing `given` came back as `["Jane Q"]` instead of `["Jane", "Q"]`, not by a later review). `CcdReverseBuilder` gets the identical shape of coverage: `test_ccd_round_trip_produces_a_convertible_document_again` runs the real `ccd_basic.xml` fixture forward, reverse, then forward again, asserting Patient name/gender/birthDate/address, Encounter class/period, and both Conditions' codes all match the original - including a dedicated assertion that the patient's real `urn:oid:2.16.840.1.113883.19.5` identifier system round-trips byte-for-byte rather than collapsing into `_reverse_identifier_root`'s own disclosed placeholder (the regression test for a real fidelity bug caught and fixed during this slice's own development, before any review - an initial version hardcoded every `<id root="...">` to a fixed placeholder string regardless of the source `Identifier.system`, silently discarding a real OID every time one was present), plus missing-Patient (`MappingError`) and Encounter/Problems-optional cases. Its own Medications breadth pass gets `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_coding_system_not_just_code` (the direct regression test for the `_build_cd_attrs` fidelity bug, confirming `Condition.code.coding[0].system` stays `http://snomed.info/sct`, not `urn:oid:http://snomed.info/sct`, across a full round trip) and `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_medication_fields` (status/intent/medication code+system/route/dose/period/patientInstruction checked against the real `ccd_medications_basic.xml` fixture's own two entries - one structured-dosing, one free-text-SIG - not just "some entries were produced"), plus `test_ccd_round_trip_produces_no_medications_section_when_negated` (a negated entry converts to no `MedicationRequest` at all, so the reverse builder must correctly emit no Medications section, not an empty one) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_ccd()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. Its own Allergies breadth pass gets `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_allergy_fields` (allergen code+system/type/category/clinicalStatus/criticality/onset/recordedDate/reaction manifestation+severity all checked against the real `ccd_allergies_basic.xml` fixture's own single, fully-populated entry - the deepest single-fixture assertion of any CCD reverse slice so far) and `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_no_known_allergies_negation` (the fully-unresolvable-allergen negation case round-trips *exactly*, `code.text == "No known allergies"` with no coding on both sides - the one negation shape this builder can fully recover, as opposed to the further-lossy still-resolvable-allergen case its own module docstring discloses but doesn't have a dedicated test for, since there's no way to assert an intentionally-approximate result against a single "correct" value), plus the same 300-seed fuzz sweep already covering every CCD section extending to this one for free. Its own Immunizations breadth pass gets `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_immunization_fields` (status/vaccine code+system/lot number/route/dose all checked against the real `ccd_immunizations_basic.xml` fixture's own two EVN-mood entries - administered and negated-refusal - with a dedicated assertion that the fixture's third, `INT`-mood planned entry never appears in either the original or round-tripped Bundle), plus the same 300-seed fuzz sweep already covering every CCD section extending to this one too. Its own Vital Signs breadth pass gets `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_vital_signs_panel_and_members` (the panel's own two-member `.hasMember` count survives on both sides, and each member's code/value/unit/interpretation checked against the real `ccd_vitals_basic.xml` fixture's own original values - the direct proof this app's second grouping-relationship reversal, after ORU's `DiagnosticReport`/`Observation`, actually reconstructs the grouping and not just a flat entry list), plus the same 300-seed fuzz sweep already covering every CCD section extending to this one too. Its own Results breadth pass gets `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_result_report_and_observations` (report code/status/result-count, and both member observations' own values - one `PQ` with a `referenceRange`, one free-text `ST` - all checked against the real `ccd_results_basic.xml` fixture's own original values), plus the same 300-seed fuzz sweep extending to this section too. Its own Procedures breadth pass - the section that completes every general-purpose CCD section on the reverse side - gets `test_ccd_round_trip_preserves_procedure_fields` (status/code/performedDateTime-or-Period/bodySite/identifier all checked against the real `ccd_procedures_basic.xml` fixture's own two entries, including a dedicated assertion that the negated entry's own root-only identifier - `Identifier.value == "urn:oid:..."`, `.system == "urn:ietf:rfc:3986"` - round-trips through `_reverse_generic_identifier`'s own fuller three-shape reversal, not just the simpler root+extension shape every earlier identifier reversal in this app only ever needed), plus the same 300-seed fuzz sweep extending to this section too. `DischargeSummaryReverseBuilder` gets `test_discharge_summary_round_trip_produces_a_convertible_document_again` (the real `discharge_summary_basic.xml` fixture's own resource-type set - Encounter/Condition/MedicationRequest - survives a full round trip, with Encounter class/period and every Condition's own code checked exactly) plus `test_discharge_summary_round_trip_splits_discharge_diagnosis_from_plain_problems` (the discharge-diagnosis Condition's own code lands inside the regenerated Hospital Discharge Diagnosis Act specifically, not the plain Concern Act, `category` survives exactly on both Conditions, and the Discharge Medications-sourced `MedicationRequest` still survives via the plain Medications section with its own `10183-2` LOINC code never regenerated) and `test_discharge_summary_missing_patient_raises_mapping_error`, plus `test_ccd_round_trip_never_splits_out_hospital_discharge_diagnosis_section` (confirming `CcdReverseBuilder`'s own default `include_discharge_specific_sections=False` never regenerates the Hospital Discharge Diagnosis templateId) and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_discharge_summary()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures, including a per-seed category-presence-set comparison. `HistoryAndPhysicalReverseBuilder` gets the equivalent coverage: `test_history_and_physical_round_trip_produces_a_convertible_document_again` (the real `history_and_physical_basic.xml` fixture's own resource-type set - Patient/Procedure - survives a full round trip, with the Procedure's own code/status checked exactly) plus `test_history_and_physical_missing_patient_raises_mapping_error` and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_history_and_physical()` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. `Edi270Builder` gets the same shape of coverage across both real 270 fixtures: `test_edi_270_round_trip_with_dependent_produces_a_convertible_interchange_again` (payer/provider identifiers, both patients' names, and - the one assertion specific to this builder's own new cross-cutting logic - that the dependent, not the subscriber, still resolves as `CoverageEligibilityRequest.patient`/`Coverage.beneficiary` after a full reverse-then-forward round trip) and `test_edi_270_round_trip_without_dependent` (the single-Patient path, where subscriber and beneficiary must resolve to the same person), plus missing-Patient and missing-payer `MappingError` cases. `Edi271Builder` gets the equivalent coverage against both real 271 fixtures: `test_edi_271_round_trip_produces_a_convertible_interchange_again` (insurance item descriptions/`inforce` survive the round trip) and `test_edi_271_rejection_round_trips_outcome_and_disposition` (the rejected fixture's `.outcome == "error"` survives exactly, `.disposition` is regenerated rather than byte-exact - see `edi_271.py`'s own disclosed one-way-gap docstring), plus the same missing-Patient/missing-payer cases. A 200-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_270()`/`generate_271()` output each run reverse-then-forward (mirroring the same fuzz discipline `app/dedup.py`'s own manual sweep already established) confirmed zero failures across 400 total seeds before this phase shipped - not committed as a standing test for the same reason dedup's own sweep isn't. `Edi276Builder`/`Edi277Builder` get `test_edi_276_round_trip_preserves_tasks_for_subscriber_and_dependent` (both real fixture Tasks' `"requested"` status survive, and - the one assertion specific to this builder's own new cross-cutting logic - that both the subscriber's and the dependent's own `Patient.id` still resolve as a referenced `Task.for_fhir` after a full reverse-then-forward round trip) and `test_edi_277_round_trip_preserves_business_status` (both real fixture Tasks' `businessStatus` category+status codes match exactly), plus `test_edi_277_error_status_round_trips_failed_status` (the dedicated `edi_277_error_status.x12` fixture's `"E1"` category confirms `Task.status == "failed"` survives) and `test_edi_276_missing_task_raises_mapping_error`/`test_edi_277_missing_task_raises_mapping_error`. A 400-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 2 triggers) of `generate("EDI", trigger, seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward confirmed zero failures the same way 270/271's own sweep did. `Edi278RequestBuilder`/`Edi278ResponseBuilder` get `test_edi_278_request_round_trip_preserves_claim_and_diagnoses` (both diagnosis codes+systems checked against the real `edi_278_request_basic.x12` fixture) and `test_edi_278_request_with_dependent_round_trip_preserves_both_patients` (the direct regression test for the `include_id=False`-copied-unexamined-from-270 bug this slice's own development caught and fixed - both the subscriber's and dependent's own member ids must survive, not just the subscriber's), plus `test_edi_278_response_round_trip_preserves_outcome_and_auth_ref`/`test_edi_278_response_denied_round_trips_partial_certification_data` (both real response fixtures' `outcome`/`preAuthRef` match exactly) and `test_edi_278_request_missing_claim_raises_mapping_error`/`test_edi_278_response_missing_claim_raises_mapping_error`. A 400-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 2 triggers) of `generate("EDI", trigger, seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward confirmed zero failures the same way. `Edi835Builder` gets `test_edi_835_round_trip_preserves_payment_and_details` (payment amount/date and the claim detail's own identifier/amount all checked against the real `edi_835_basic.x12` fixture, plus a dedicated regression assertion that the payer's own non-canonical `"XV"` qualifier round-trips to the same disclosed fallback system rather than silently falling back to the wrong default) and `test_edi_835_multi_claim_round_trip_preserves_all_claims` (both claims in the real `edi_835_multi_claim.x12` fixture survive), plus `test_edi_835_missing_payment_reconciliation_raises_mapping_error` and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("EDI", "835", seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. `Edi837pBuilder` gets `test_edi_837p_round_trip_preserves_claim_items_and_diagnoses` (both service lines' procedure code/charge/diagnosis-pointers/care-team-sequence/serviced-date, and both diagnoses, all checked against the real `edi_837p_basic.x12` fixture) and `test_edi_837p_with_dependent_round_trip_preserves_both_patients` (both patients from the real `edi_837p_with_dependent.x12` fixture survive by name, since this fixture's dependent - unlike 278's own - carries no member-specific id to compare instead), plus `test_edi_837p_missing_claim_raises_mapping_error` and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("EDI", "837P", seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. `Edi837iBuilder` gets `test_edi_837i_round_trip_preserves_claim_items_and_discharge_status` (both service lines' revenue code/procedure/charge/care-team-sequence/serviced-date, all three diagnoses, and the discharge status all checked against the real `edi_837i_basic.x12` fixture, plus a dedicated assertion that `item.diagnosisSequence` stays `None` - confirming this builder never fabricates a diagnosis-pointer composite `SV2` genuinely doesn't have) and `test_edi_837i_with_dependent_round_trip_preserves_revenue_code_only_item` (the real `edi_837i_with_dependent.x12` fixture's own text-only "Revenue code X" fallback item, with no procedure composite at all, survives exactly - not just the coded case), plus `test_edi_837i_missing_claim_raises_mapping_error` and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("EDI", "837I", seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. `Edi837dBuilder` gets `test_edi_837d_round_trip_preserves_tooth_information_and_no_diagnosis` (both items' procedure code/system/charge/care-team-sequence/serviced-date all checked against the real `edi_837d_basic.x12` fixture - which deliberately carries zero diagnoses and relies entirely on the claim-level `DTP*472` default for both service lines - plus a dedicated assertion that the first item's tooth `bodySite`/`subSite` survive while the second item's stay unset, confirming the `TOO` segment's own per-line placement round-trips correctly) and `test_edi_837d_no_dependent_round_trip_preserves_diagnosis_pointer` (the real `edi_837d_no_dependent.x12` fixture's own resolvable diagnosis and `SV3-11` pointer, plus its per-line `DTP*472`, survive exactly), plus `test_edi_837d_missing_claim_raises_mapping_error` and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate_837d(rng)` output run reverse-then-forward with zero failures - the last EDI family this pillar had left, completing the full "big five" HIPAA suite's reverse-direction test coverage. The five additional ADT triggers get a parametrized round-trip test (`test_adt_trigger_round_trips_and_preserves_class_and_status`, covering A02/A04/A05 - each asserting the correct `EVN-1`/`MSH-9` trigger code and that `Encounter.class`/`.status` survive a full round trip) plus dedicated tests for the two triggers with real extra behavior: `test_adt_a02_round_trips_prior_and_current_location` (both `status="completed"`/`"active"` location entries survive), and `test_adt_a03_round_trips_discharge_disposition_and_status`/`test_adt_a03_missing_discharge_time_raises_mapping_error` (mirroring the forward `AdtA03Mapper`'s own required-discharge-time test coverage) and `test_adt_a08_round_trips_inferred_status` (parametrized over both the finished and in-progress real A08 fixtures, direct proof the "no A08-specific code needed" design claim actually holds for both branches). A 600-seed fuzz sweep (100 seeds × 6 triggers) of `generate("ADT", trigger, seed=...)` output run reverse-then-forward confirmed zero failures the same way. The three cancel triggers get `test_adt_cancel_trigger_round_trips_and_preserves_entered_in_error_status` (parametrized over A11/A13/A38 against their own real fixtures - each confirming `status="entered-in-error"` and `period.start` survive exactly, direct proof the EVN-2-is-inert-on-cancel-triggers design claim holds) and `test_adt_a13_round_trips_discharge_disposition`, plus `test_list_supported_targets_includes_all_three_adt_cancel_triggers` and a 600-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 3 triggers) of `generate("ADT", trigger, seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward confirming zero failures. `SiuS12Builder` gets `test_siu_s12_round_trip_preserves_appointment_fields` (status/start/end/duration/reasonCode/appointmentType/serviceType/comment/participant-count all checked against the real `siu_s12_basic.hl7` fixture's own original values, not just "some text was produced") and `test_siu_s12_round_trip_preserves_practitioner_location_and_device` (all three non-patient participant resource types survive with their own real field values - `Practitioner.name`, `Device.deviceName`/`.type`), plus missing-Patient/missing-Appointment `MappingError` cases and a 200-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("SIU", "S12", seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. The five additional SIU triggers get a parametrized round-trip test (`test_siu_trigger_round_trips_and_preserves_status`, covering S13/S14/S15/S17/S26 against their own real fixtures - each asserting the correct `MSH-9` trigger code and that `Appointment.status` survives a full round trip) plus `test_siu_s26_missing_start_time_raises_mapping_error` (the one assertion specific to this breadth pass's own real per-trigger difference - a booked/no-show-family trigger with no resolvable `Appointment.start` can't round-trip, mirroring `AdtA03Builder`'s own required-discharge-time precedent) and a 1200-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 6 triggers) of `generate("SIU", trigger, seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. `OruR01Builder` gets the same shape of coverage: `test_oru_r01_round_trip_preserves_report_and_observation_grouping` (both reports' status/result-count checked against the real `oru_r01_basic.hl7` fixture, and - the one assertion specific to this builder's own new grouping logic - that each report's regenerated `OBX` segments resolve back to only its own original `Observation`s, not a flat scan of every result in the message) and `test_oru_r01_round_trip_preserves_observation_values_and_performer` (value/units/status/interpretation/effective-time/OBX-16 performer all checked against their exact original values), plus `test_oru_r01_missing_patient_raises_mapping_error`/`test_oru_r01_missing_diagnostic_report_raises_mapping_error` and a 200-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("ORU", "R01", seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. The four additional ORU triggers get a parametrized round-trip test (`test_oru_trigger_round_trips_and_preserves_report_grouping`, covering R30/R31/R32/R40 against their own real fixtures - each asserting the correct `MSH-9` trigger code and that every report's code/result-count survive a full round trip) plus `test_list_supported_targets_includes_all_five_oru_triggers` and a 1000-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 5 triggers) of `generate("ORU", trigger, seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward confirming zero failures the same way the ADT breadth pass did. `MdmT02Builder` gets the same shape of coverage: `test_mdm_t02_round_trip_preserves_document_fields` (type/master-identifier/date/description/security-label checked against the real `mdm_t02_basic.hl7` fixture, plus both originator/authenticator Practitioners' own real names) and `test_mdm_t02_round_trip_preserves_document_body` (the referenced `Binary`'s own decoded byte content matches exactly, not just "some OBX segments were produced"), plus `test_mdm_t02_same_author_authenticator_dedups_practitioner_on_round_trip` (the one assertion specific to this builder's own dedup-preserving behavior - a single materialized Practitioner referenced by both `author` and `authenticator` stays a single Practitioner, not two, after a full round trip), `test_mdm_t02_missing_patient_raises_mapping_error`/`test_mdm_t02_missing_document_reference_raises_mapping_error`, and a 300-seed fuzz sweep of `generate("MDM", "T02", seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward with zero failures. The five additional MDM triggers get a parametrized round-trip test (`test_mdm_trigger_round_trips_and_preserves_document_type`, covering T04/T06/T08/T10/T11 - each asserting the correct `MSH-9` trigger code and that `DocumentReference.masterIdentifier` survives a full round trip) built against real `generate("MDM", trigger, seed=7)` output rather than a dedicated fixture (unlike ORU's own breadth pass, no hand-built per-trigger MDM fixture exists), plus `test_list_supported_targets_includes_all_six_mdm_triggers` and a 1200-seed fuzz sweep (200 seeds × 6 triggers) of `generate("MDM", trigger, seed=...)` run reverse-then-forward confirming zero failures. `tests/test_routes.py` covers `/api/transform`'s full error-shape matrix (invalid JSON → 400, invalid Bundle → 422 "Invalid FHIR Bundle", missing Patient → 422 "Mapping error", unregistered target → 422), the no-JS `/transform` form fallback rendering both a successful result and an error in the page, all thirty-nine targets appearing in the dropdown (including `test_list_supported_targets_includes_oru_r01`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_all_five_oru_triggers`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_mdm_t02`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_all_six_mdm_triggers`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_all_six_siu_triggers`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_all_three_adt_cancel_triggers`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_discharge_summary`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_history_and_physical`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_276_and_277`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_278_request_and_response`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_835`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_837p`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_837i`/`test_index_transform_target_dropdown_includes_837d`) (plus a dedicated assertion that CCD's own trigger-less label renders as `"CDA CCD"` with no stray trailing `^`), and `test_convert_then_transform_round_trips_end_to_end` - the full realistic user flow (convert a real fixture, transform that exact Bundle back out, convert the result a second time) exercised through the actual HTTP layer, not just the underlying Python functions. The "Use Bundle above" button visibility bug (see `app/transform/`'s own bullet above) was caught by driving the real running app with Puppeteer, not by any of the route-level tests above - none of them render `app/static/app.js` at all, so a bug in client-side pane-visibility toggling was invisible to them by construction; this is disclosed here as a reminder that this app's UI-behavior surface (as opposed to its HTTP-response-shape surface) needs an actual browser to verify, the same "test the feature in a browser" discipline this project's own contributing guidance already calls for on any UI-affecting change.


**Data Specification testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder.py`, `tests/test_data_specification_routes.py`) mirrors the same discipline as every other pillar's own first slice. `test_provenance_recording_does_not_change_bundle_output` is the load-bearing one - parametrized across a representative fixture for all 9 ADT triggers (both branches for A08 and A13, the two triggers whose own docstrings disclose a real behavioral split) - patches `uuid.uuid4` to an identical deterministic sequence for an untraced and a traced call to the same mapper, then asserts `bundle.model_dump(exclude_none=True)` is byte-identical between the two: instrumentation adding a `.record()`/`.record_inferred()` call must never change what a mapper actually builds, and this is the one test that would catch it if it ever did. Direct content-correctness tests check exact expected crosswalk entries against `adt_a01_basic.hl7`'s own already-known values (the same fixture `tests/test_adt_a01_mapping.py` itself asserts against, so the two test files can never silently drift apart about what this fixture actually contains), plus three dedicated regression tests for the real complications the mapper-layer instrumentation bullet above discloses: `test_adt_a02_transfer_reindexes_location_provenance_after_insert` (both `location[0]`/`location[1]` facts point at their correct, post-insert source fields, with no stray third fact left over), `test_adt_a11_cancel_admit_never_leaks_evn2_period_start_into_crosswalk` (the exact same EVN-2-mislabeling hazard `test_adt_a11_mapping.py::test_cancel_admit_without_pv1_44_does_not_leak_evn2_as_period_start` already guards against on the Bundle side, checked here against the crosswalk's own facts instead), and `test_adt_a13_with_discharge_period_start_points_at_pv1_44_not_evn2` (confirms the corrected fact, not the stale pre-correction one, survives into the resolved crosswalk). `resolve_bundle_paths`/`ProvenanceRecorder` get direct unit tests in isolation: the `bundle.id`-special-case (no `entry[N].resource.` prefix), the defensive skip-not-crash path for a `resource_id` that never made it into the final Bundle, and `record()`'s own last-write-wins semantics (`forget()`/`forget_prefix()` included) - all four exercised against small hand-built `Bundle`/`ProvenanceRecorder` objects, not real fixtures, the same "don't reuse generator/fixture output for helper-specific unit tests" discipline `test_dedup.py` already established. `tests/test_data_specification_routes.py` covers the route/API layer the same way `test_routes.py` covers every other feature: the page renders with a working nav link back to `/`, `/` itself gained a nav link forward to `/data-specification`, `/api/data-specification` against the real `adt_a01_basic.hl7` fixture returns a fully-supported report with spot-checked entries (including confirming a `derivation="inferred"` entry has no `source_location` key at all once serialized with `exclude_none=True`, not a `null` one), the real EDI fixture (`edi_270_basic.x12`) converts successfully but reports `unsupported=True` with an empty `entries` list (X12's own `unsupported=True` example - no instrumentation exists for that format at all yet), the real C-CDA fixture (`ccd_basic.xml`) converts successfully and reports `unsupported=True` too but with a genuinely non-empty `entries` list once the header/Problems instrumentation shipped (see the mapper-layer bullet above for why no CDA document type is ever added to a "fully supported" set) - plus dedicated `test_api_data_specification_siu_type_is_now_instrumented`/`test_api_data_specification_oru_type_is_now_instrumented`/`test_api_data_specification_mdm_type_is_now_instrumented` regressions proving SIU/ORU/MDM all stay reported as supported (the file's own dedicated "non-instrumented HL7v2 type" regression test, previously re-pointed at each newly-un-instrumented type in turn as SIU/ORU/MDM each shipped, was retired once no such HL7v2 example remained to point it at - its job is now fully covered by the EDI/C-CDA cases plus these three regressions), a malformed-input case maps to the same 400 "Parse error" shape as every other feature, and both the no-JS form-post success and error paths render into the page. Verified end-to-end with a real browser (Puppeteer, installed to the session scratch dir per this project's own established practice, not a project dependency) before considering this phase done, not just via these route-level tests - see the architecture bullet above for what that pass specifically checked and why a genuinely separate page was chosen partly *because* it sidesteps needing this kind of manual verification for cross-page pane interference.

**MDM provenance testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder.py`) extends the same discipline to MDM, the fourth and final HL7v2 message type: `test_mdm_provenance_recording_does_not_change_bundle_output` is parametrized across all 4 real MDM fixtures for T02 (basic, minimal, obx-with-caret, same-author-authenticator) plus the basic fixture reused across all 5 remaining triggers (T04/T06/T08/T10/T11), the identical byte-for-byte Bundle-output-unchanged proof every earlier slice's own version already established. `test_mdm_t02_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` checks exact expected entries against `mdm_t02_basic.hl7`'s own full TXA shape - status (hardcoded/inferred, never read from TXA-19's actual value), contentType/type/masterIdentifier/date/securityLabel/description all direct from their own TXA fields, both the author/authenticator display strings and the originator Practitioner's own name (proving the "one field, two destinations" case), and the joined two-OBX-line `Binary.data` fact (with its own `"(×2 segments)"` disclosed location). Three dedicated regression tests cover the real complications the mapper-layer instrumentation bullet above discloses: `test_mdm_t02_same_author_authenticator_records_authenticator_display_from_txa10` (the deduped-Practitioner case's own `authenticator.display` fact must still point at TXA-10, not TXA-9, even though both reference the identical underlying resource), `test_mdm_t02_obx_with_caret_binary_data_is_not_truncated_in_crosswalk` (the crosswalk's own recorded `Binary.data` value must match the untruncated `raw_field_str` text, not a caret-truncated one), and `test_mdm_t02_minimal_fixture_without_pv1_records_no_encounter_facts` (no PV1 segment means `build_minimal_encounter` never runs, so zero Encounter-scoped facts anywhere in the resolved crosswalk, confirmed via the same no-orphan-fact check every earlier slice's own zero-behavior-change test already runs).

**C-CDA provenance testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder_cda.py`, a new file - see that file's own docstring for why it's kept separate from `test_provenance_recorder.py` rather than appended, mirroring `test_cda_validation.py`'s own precedent) extends the same discipline to the first non-HL7v2 format: `test_cda_provenance_recording_does_not_change_bundle_output` is parametrized across 16 real fixtures spanning all three document types, including several whose own section (Medications/Allergies/Vitals/Results/Procedures/Immunizations) is deliberately NOT instrumented this slice - direct proof the no-op `recorder=None` additions across those 8 sibling `SECTION_BUILDERS` files don't alter their own output either, not just the two (header, Problems) that do real work. `test_ccd_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` checks exact expected entries against `ccd_basic.xml`'s own already-known values (the same fixture `test_ccd_mapping.py` itself asserts against). Four dedicated regression tests cover the real complications the mapper-layer instrumentation bullet above discloses, none of which any earlier (HL7v2) slice needed to handle: `test_ccd_header_multiplicities_records_correct_indices_for_repeating_fields` (two `<name>`s/`<addr>`s/`<telecom>`s/encounter `<id>`s - every kept FHIR array index and its XML repetition index must both be correct, including a root-only patient `<id>`'s own `urn:oid:` fallback value still producing a real fact rather than a skipped one), `test_ccd_effective_time_variants_records_bare_value_vs_low_high_shapes` (all three legal `IVL_TS` shapes - bare `@value`, `<low>`-only, `<low>`+`<high>`, `nullFlavor` - each producing the correct, shape-specific `source_location` via the new `effective_time_location` helper, not a guessed default), `test_ccd_problem_status_observation_overrides_act_status_records_nested_location` (the nested Status Observation's own deeper location wins over the Concern Act's own `statusCode` location when both could apply, mirroring `test_ccd_mapping.py`'s identical Bundle-side "resolved value wins" assertion), and `test_ccd_problem_negated_produces_no_condition_facts` (a `negationInd="true"` entry produces zero Condition-scoped facts anywhere in the resolved crosswalk, not just an empty Condition). `test_ccd_medications_section_records_no_facts_yet` is the direct proof for `ccd_medications_basic.xml` specifically that a real `MedicationRequest` still gets built (conversion unaffected) while carrying zero recorded facts, confirming the "accept recorder, no-op" contract holds for a section that hasn't shipped its own slice yet. `test_discharge_summary_hospital_discharge_diagnosis_condition_also_recorded` is the direct regression proof for the "for free" `build_hospital_discharge_diagnoses` extension the mapper-layer bullet discloses - both the discharge-diagnosis Condition (`category="encounter-diagnosis"`) and the plain-Problems Condition in the same `discharge_summary_basic.xml` document get real, independently-resolved `code.coding[0].code` facts. `test_history_and_physical_header_only_fixture_records_header_facts` confirms a document with no Problems section at all (an H&P-specific narrative section this app doesn't recognize instead) still produces correct header-only facts, no crash and no spurious Condition-scoped ones.

**EDI provenance testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`, a new file, the same "own file per format" precedent `test_provenance_recorder_cda.py` already established) extends the same discipline to the third and final input format: `test_edi_provenance_recording_does_not_change_bundle_output` is parametrized across all 4 real 270/271 fixtures, all 4 real 276/277 fixtures, plus fixtures from the remaining still-uninstrumented EDI families (278, 835, 837P) - direct proof both the real instrumentation AND the no-op `recorder=None` additions across the other builder files (plus the "free" `assemble_bundle` facts they now get) don't alter any of their own output. `test_edi_270_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` checks exact expected entries against `edi_270_basic.x12`'s own already-known values (the same fixture `test_eligibility_270_mapping.py` itself asserts against) - payer/subscriber/dependent name and identifier facts, the inferred `status`/`purpose[0]`, the direct `item[0].coding[0].code`/`servicedDate`/`created`, and both `Bundle.identifier`/`.timestamp`. `test_edi_271_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` does the same for both of that fixture's own EB groups, each resolving to its own independently-indexed `insurance[0].item[i]` facts - including the first boolean fact this pillar has ever recorded (`insurance[0].inforce`), confirmed stringified (`"True"`/`"False"`) per `ProvenanceEntry.value`'s own `str | None` type rather than assumed. Three dedicated regression tests cover the real complications the mapper-layer instrumentation bullet above discloses: `test_edi_271_rejected_records_direct_outcome_and_disposition` (a real `AAA01="N"` rejection flips `.outcome`/`.disposition` from their own inferred defaults to direct facts pointing at `AAA-1`/`AAA-3`, while `.inforce` - genuinely independent of the rejection - still resolves to its own inferred `False`), `test_edi_270_no_dependent_records_only_subscriber_facts` (no 2000D loop at all means exactly one Patient's worth of name/birthDate/gender facts, not two, with the standard no-orphan-fact check), and `test_edi_278_non_instrumented_family_still_gets_free_bundle_level_facts`/`test_edi_835_non_instrumented_family_with_no_bht_gets_zero_facts` proving the "free"-facts design itself (every resolved fact for a still-uninstrumented BHT-bearing family is `Bundle.identifier`/`.timestamp`-scoped, nothing resource-specific; 835's own lack of a BHT segment means it doesn't even get those free facts - `entries == []` despite a real, multi-resource Bundle being built).

**EDI 276/277 provenance testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`) extends the same discipline to the second EDI family: `test_edi_276_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` checks both of that fixture's own Tasks (subscriber's and dependent's) resolve `status="requested"`/`intent="order"`/`code.text="Claim Status"` as `derivation="inferred"` with no `businessStatus` fact at all - direct proof 276's own request-side Task never fabricates STC-sourced facts it doesn't have. `test_edi_277_basic_records_direct_status_and_business_status` checks the sibling response fixture's own two STC-populated Tasks resolve `status` as `derivation="direct"` from `STC-1.1`, with both `businessStatus.coding[]` entries pointing at their own correct sub-component locations (`STC-1.1`/`STC-1.2`). `test_edi_277_error_status_records_failed_status` is the dedicated regression proof that `edi_277_error_status.x12`'s own `"E1"` category resolves directly to `Task.status="failed"` via `STC_CATEGORY_PREFIX_TO_TASK_STATUS`'s own `"E"` entry, not the `"completed"`/inferred default a missing-STC case would produce.

**EDI 278 provenance testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`) extends the same discipline to the third EDI family: `test_edi_278_request_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` checks the real request fixture's own inferred `status`/`use`/`type.coding[0].code`/`priority.text`/`item[0].productOrService.text` (no `UM` segment present) alongside its two direct, independently-indexed `diagnosis[]` facts from one `HI` segment's own two composites. `test_edi_278_response_certified_records_direct_outcome_and_item_category` checks the sibling response fixture (whose own `UM` segment IS present, unlike the basic request) resolves `item[0].productOrService` as a direct fact from `UM-3` instead, plus the `ClaimResponse`'s own direct `outcome`/`preAuthRef`/adjudication facts from `HCR` - including a dedicated assertion that `Claim.created` and `ClaimResponse.created` resolve to the *identical* displayed string, the direct regression proof for the `datetime`-vs-string formatting bug the mapper-layer bullet above discloses catching before commit. `test_edi_278_response_denied_records_two_adjudication_entries` confirms both `HCR01`(action)/`HCR03`(reason) codes resolve to their own separately-indexed `adjudication[]` entries when both are present, with no spurious `preAuthRef` fact when `HCR02` is genuinely absent. `test_edi_278_request_with_dependent_records_both_patients` confirms the with-dependent fixture's own two Patients (subscriber and dependent) both carry their own independently-resolved name facts, not just one.

**EDI 835 provenance testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`) extends the same discipline to the fourth EDI family: `test_edi_835_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` checks the real basic fixture's own payer/payee Organization facts (from `N1`), the `PaymentReconciliation`'s own inferred `status` alongside its direct `created`/`paymentDate` (both from the identical `BPR16` source, confirmed to share the same value and location) and `paymentAmount.value` (from `BPR02`), its one `detail[0]` entry's own `type`/`identifier`/`amount` facts (from `CLP2`/`CLP1`/`CLP4`), `Bundle.identifier` (from `TRN02`, this family's only header identifier source), and a dedicated assertion that `Bundle.timestamp` is genuinely absent from the resolved crosswalk - 835's own disclosed permanent gap (no full date+time field exists anywhere in this transaction set), not an oversight. `test_edi_835_multi_claim_records_independently_indexed_details` confirms both of that fixture's own `CLP` groups (one paid, one denied with a `$0.00` `CLP04`) resolve to their own, correctly-indexed `detail[0]`/`detail[1]` facts - including confirming the zero-but-present paid amount is recorded, not silently skipped the way a genuinely absent value would be.

**EDI 837P provenance testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`) extends the same discipline to the fifth EDI family, and the first covering a `Claim.item[]` shape: `test_edi_837p_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` checks the real basic fixture's own two service lines' procedure code/charge/diagnosis-pointers/care-team-sequence/serviced-date facts, both diagnoses, and the claim's own inferred `status`/`type`/`use`/`priority` alongside its direct `identifier`/`total`/`created` - including a dedicated assertion that `item[0]`'s own `diagnosisSequence` fact resolves to the joined string `"1,2"` (both pointers) while `item[1]`'s resolves to `"1"` (a single pointer), and that both items' own `locationCodeableConcept` facts independently point at the identical claim-wide `CLM-5.1` source. `test_edi_837p_with_dependent_records_both_patients` confirms the with-dependent fixture's own two Patients (subscriber and dependent) both carry their own independently-resolved name facts, and - since that fixture's own claim has no rendering provider at all - that no `careTeam` fact is recorded, proving the inferred `role` fact is genuinely conditional on the provider's presence, not unconditionally emitted.

**EDI 837I provenance testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder_edi.py`) extends the same discipline to the sixth EDI family, and the dedicated regression proof for the `segment_repetition` fix: `test_edi_837i_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` checks the real basic fixture's own three diagnoses - resolving `diagnosis[0]` (from the Principal `HI` segment) and `diagnosis[1]`/`diagnosis[2]` (from the Other `HI` segment) to three genuinely distinct `edi_location("HI", ..., segment_repetition=...)` source locations, with a dedicated assertion that `diagnosis[0]`'s and `diagnosis[1]`'s own locations are NOT equal despite both resolving from intra-segment position 1 - plus the claim's own inferred `type="institutional"`, the discharge-status `supportingInfo[0]` entry's own inferred `category`/direct `code` (from `CL1-3`), the first service line's own direct `productOrService`/`revenue` facts (from `SV2-2.2`/`SV2-1`), and the inferred `careTeam[0].role`. `test_edi_837i_with_dependent_records_text_fallback_for_procedure_less_line` is the dedicated regression proof for the new three-way `productOrService` branch 837I's own service lines need that 837P's don't: the with-dependent fixture's own single line has no procedure composite, only a revenue code, so `productOrService.text` must resolve as a *direct* fact (`"Revenue code 0120"`, built from the real `SV2-1` value) rather than the fully-synthetic `"Unspecified service"` an *inferred* fallback would produce - confirming the crosswalk correctly distinguishes "a real value, just reformatted" from "no real value at all," the same distinction this pillar's own established `derivation` field exists to make.

**ORU provenance testing** (`tests/test_provenance_recorder.py`) extends the same discipline to ORU: `test_oru_provenance_recording_does_not_change_bundle_output` is parametrized across all 8 real ORU fixtures spanning all 5 triggers (R01's own basic/minimal/shared-performer/caret-in-free-text edge cases, plus one basic fixture each for R30/R31/R32/R40), the identical byte-for-byte Bundle-output-unchanged proof every earlier slice's own version already established. `test_oru_r01_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` checks exact expected entries against `oru_r01_basic.hl7`'s own two-report, three-observation, one-performer shape - both reports' own independently-resolved status/code/effectiveDateTime/issued facts (proving the per-loop-iteration id-hoisting doesn't cross-contaminate which report a fact belongs to), the WBC observation's own valueQuantity.value/.unit/referenceRange/interpretation/effectiveDateTime facts, and the OBX-16 performer's own identifier/name facts. Three dedicated regression tests cover the real complications the mapper-layer instrumentation bullet above discloses: `test_oru_r01_shared_performer_recorded_once_not_once_per_observation` (a cache-hit Practitioner must not produce a second, redundant `name[0].family` fact), `test_oru_r01_free_text_value_with_caret_is_not_truncated_in_crosswalk` (the crosswalk's own recorded `valueString` must match the untruncated `raw_field_str` value, not a caret-truncated one), and `test_oru_minimal_fixture_without_pv1_records_no_encounter_facts` (no PV1 segment means `build_minimal_encounter` never runs, so zero Encounter-scoped facts should appear anywhere in the resolved crosswalk - confirmed via the same `len(entries) == len(recorder.facts)` no-orphan-fact check every earlier slice's own zero-behavior-change test already runs).


**SIU provenance testing** (appended to `tests/test_provenance_recorder.py`, not a new file - both formats share the identical deterministic-uuid zero-behavior-change harness) mirrors ADT's own shape at the same depth: `test_siu_provenance_recording_does_not_change_bundle_output` parametrized across 13 real fixtures spanning all 6 triggers (including the AIG-as-Location, AIP-id-only, multiple-NTE, partial-TQ1, SCH-11-fallback, and stale-SCH-9-duration edge-case fixtures the forward mapper's own test suite already established) - the same byte-for-byte `model_dump(exclude_none=True)` comparison proving instrumentation changes nothing. `test_siu_s12_basic_crosswalk_matches_known_field_values` is the one test this slice needed that ADT's own equivalent didn't: a direct assertion that AIP-3 produces *two* independent entries (the materialized `Practitioner.name[0].family` and the `Appointment.participant[1].actor.display` string) sharing the identical `source_location`, proving the "one source field, two FHIR destinations" case is actually captured, not just designed for. `test_siu_s12_sch11_fallback_timing_points_at_sch_not_tq1`/`test_siu_s13_duration_prefers_tq1_over_stale_sch9` are the dedicated regression tests for `_timing_source_location`/`_tq1_duration_used`'s own dual-source resolution, mirroring `test_adt_a13_with_discharge_period_start_points_at_pv1_44_not_evn2`'s exact role for ADT's analogous PV1-44-or-EVN-2 case. `test_siu_s12_aig_device_records_type_and_identifier` confirms the AIG-derived `Device`'s own `type`/`identifier` facts resolve correctly against that specific resource's own `Bundle.entry[N]` index, not the Appointment's.
